{"batchcomplete":true,"query":{"redirects":[{"from":"UFC on Fuel TV: Sanchez vs. Ellenberger","to":"2012 in UFC","tofragment":"UFC on Fuel TV: Sanchez vs. Ellenberger"}],"pages":[{"pageid":33604711,"ns":0,"title":"UFC 144","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts event in 2012}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=UFC 144: Edgar vs. Henderson\n|image=UFC144Poster.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date=February 26, 2012\n|venue=[[Saitama Super Arena]]\n|city=[[Saitama, Saitama|Saitama]], [[Japan]]\n|attendance=21,000 (paid: 18,000)\n|buyrate=375,000{{cite web |url=http://www.mmatorch.com/artman2/publish/UFC_2/article_12658.shtml|title=UFC 144 early pay-per-view estimates reportedly at an impressive 375,000 buys|publisher=mmatorch.com |access-date=2012-02-29}}\n|previousevent= [[2012 in UFC#UFC on Fuel TV: Sanchez vs. Ellenberger|UFC on Fuel TV: Sanchez vs. Ellenberger]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC on FX: Alves vs. Kampmann]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 144: Edgar vs. Henderson''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] [[pay-per-view]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on February 26, 2012, at the [[Saitama Super Arena]] in [[Saitama, Saitama|Saitama]], [[Japan]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/ufc-144#/fight|title=UFC 144: Edgar vs. Henderson|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|access-date=2011-11-28}}\n\n==Venue==\nUFC 144 was held at the Saitama Super Arena in the [[Chūō-ku, Saitama|Chūō-ku]] central ward of Saitama City in [[Saitama Prefecture]]. The sellout{{cite news|last=Non|first=Sergio|title=Henderson outpoints Edgar, wins UFC title|url=https://www.usatoday.com/sports/mma/post/2012-02-26/henderson-outpoints-edgar-wins-ufc-title/634892/1|access-date=23 April 2012|newspaper=USA Today|date=26 February 2012}} event was held in the Super Arena's[http://www.saitama-arena.co.jp/blog/2012/02/?event=1 2012年2月のイベント/UFC JAPAN] {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120416064947/http://www.saitama-arena.co.jp/blog/2012/02/?event=1 |date=2012-04-16 }}{{in lang|ja}} Main Stage Center Arena configuration,[http://www.saitama-arena.co.jp/seats_info/arena_center/ メインアリーナ – センターステージ] {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120419035426/http://www.saitama-arena.co.jp/seats_info/arena_center/ |date=2012-04-19 }}{{in lang|ja}}[http://www.saitama-arena.co.jp/e/facility.html Facility information] {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120417202609/http://www.saitama-arena.co.jp/e/facility.html |date=2012-04-17 }} with a ticket cost of between ¥5,800 and ¥100,000 (ca. US$70 – US$1250).\n\n==Background==\nUFC 144 marked the UFC's fifth appearance in Japan, their first event in Japan since ''[[UFC 29]]'' in 2000, and its first event in Japan since the 2007 purchase of [[Pride Fighting Championships]], which held many of its cards at Saitama Super Arena. The UFC event featured seven fights on the main card.Nagatsuka, Kaz, \"[http://www.japantimes.co.jp/text/sp20120228a1.html UFC hopes to shake up Japan fight scene]\", ''[[Japan Times]]'', 28 February 2012, p. 15.\n\n[[George Sotiropoulos]] was expected to face [[Takanori Gomi]] at this event, but was forced out of the bout with an injury and replaced by [[Eiji Mitsuoka]].{{cite web |url=http://mmaweekly.com/sotiropoulos-out-eiji-mitsuoka-faces-takanori-gomi-at-ufc-144|title=Sotiropoulos Out; Eiji Mitsuoka Faces Takanori Gomi at UFC 144|date=27 January 2012 |publisher=mmaweekly.com |access-date=2012-01-27}}\n\n[[Leonard Garcia]] was expected to face [[Zhang Tiequan]] at this event, but was forced out of the bout with an injury.{{cite web |url=http://mmaweekly.com/leonard-garcia-injured-and-out-of-ufc-144|title=Leonard Garcia Injured and Out of UFC 144 |date=30 January 2012 |publisher=mmaweekly.com |access-date=2012-01-30}} [[Issei Tamura]] stepped in for Garcia and fought Zhang.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/27386/with-leonard-garcia-hurt-tiequan-zhang-meets-newcomer-issei-tamura-at-ufc-144.mma|title=With Leonard Garcia hurt, Tiequan Zhang meets newcomer Issei Tamura at UFC 144|author=MMAjunkie.com Staff|date=February 11, 2012|publisher=mmajunkie.com|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120713225334/mmajunkie.com/news/27386/with-leonard-garcia-hurt-tiequan-zhang-meets-newcomer-issei-tamura-at-ufc-144.mma|archive-date=July 13, 2012}}\n\nAt the UFC 144 weigh ins, [[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]] failed to make the 206 lb weight limit and came in 5 lbs overweight at 211 lbs. Jackson was fined 20 percent of his earnings and the bout was contested at a catchweight of 211 lb.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/ufc/2012/2/24/2822889/ufc-144-weigh-in-results-edgar-vs-henderson|title=UFC 144 Weigh-In Results: Rampage Jackson Misses Weight|author=Shaun Al-Shatti|date=February martial arts television series and event in 2012}}{{Convert to Episode table}}\n\n{{Infobox television\n| image =\n| genre = [[reality television|Reality]], [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n| runtime = 60 minutes\n| creator = [[Frank Fertitta III]], [[Lorenzo Fertitta]], [[Dana White]]\n| starring = [[Dana White]], [[Jon Anik]], [[Dominick Cruz]], and [[Urijah Faber]]\n| country = United States\n| network = [[FX (TV channel)|FX]]\n| first_aired = {{Start date|2012|03|09}}\n| last_aired = {{End date|2012|06|01}}\n}}\n\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Live'''''{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/27477/video-the-ultimate-fighter-15-debuts-march-9-with-16-live-fights-on-fx.mma |title=Video: 'The Ultimate Fighter 15' debuts March 9 with 16 live fights on FX |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=February 17, 2012 |access-date=February 18, 2012}} (also known as '''''The Ultimate Fighter 15''''') was the fifteenth installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced [[reality television]] series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''. This season marked the show's debut on the [[FX (TV channel)|FX]] network, the debut of [[Jon Anik]] as the show's host,{{cite web|url=http://mma.sbnation.com/2011/10/28/2521111/ufc-jon-anik-espn-broadcast-the-ultimate-fighter-mma-news/in/2285147|title=UFC, Mixed Martial Arts (MMA) News, Results: MMA Fighting|publisher=Vox Media|work=MMA Fighting|date=28 October 2011 }} and featured [[Lightweight (MMA)|Lightweight]] fighters.\n\nAs part of the deal with FX, the show was revamped. Prior seasons of the show were filmed in six weeks, edited and aired several months after filming was completed. This season the show was being filmed over thirteen weeks and was being edited \"the week of\" before being broadcast. In addition this season was the first to feature live fights.{{cite web|url=http://www.fightofthenight.com/news/ufc-unveils-new-live-revamp-of-the-ultimate-fighter-on-fx/|title=UFC Unveils New 'Live' Revamp Of 'The Ultimate Fighter' On FX|author=Ross Cole|date=18 August 2011|work=Fight of the Night}}\n\nThe UFC and FX held open tryouts on December 5, 2011, in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]]. The casting call went out for [[Lightweight (MMA)|Lightweight]] and [[Welterweight (MMA)|Welterweight]] fighters.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/25918/the-ultimate-fighter-15-features-lightweights-welterweights-dec-5-tryouts.mma |title='The Ultimate Fighter 15' features lightweights, welterweights; Dec. 5 tryouts | MMAjunkie.com |access-date=November 3, 2011 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20111103201352/http://mmajunkie.com/news/25918/the-ultimate-fighter-15-features-lightweights-welterweights-dec-5-tryouts.mma |archive-date=November 3, 2011 }} Some of the fighters that showed up for the tryouts included UFC veteran Jamie Yager, [[World Extreme Cagefighting|WEC]] veteran [[Blas Avena]], and [[Bellator Fighting Championship|Bellator]] veterans [[Rudy Bears]], [[Zak Cummings]], and Jacob McClintock, and K-1 kickboxer Kultar Gill.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/26398/tuf-15-tryouts-attract-350-fighters-including-jamie-yager-kultar-gill-to-las-vegas.mma|title='TUF 15' tryouts attract 350 fighters, including Jamie Yager, Kultar Gill, to Las Vegas|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=December 5, 2011}}\n\nOn December 6, 2011, it was announced that the coaches for the season would be [[Dominick Cruz]] and [[Urijah Faber]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/news/tuf-15-coach-announcement-fx |title=Rivals Cruz, Faber to coach RUF 15 |date=December 6, 2011 |publisher=UFC.com |access-date=December 6, 2011}} They were set to fight for the title, until Cruz's injury on May 7, 2012. The show debuted on March 9, 2012.\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Coaches===\n{{Col-begin}}\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{color box|red|border=silver}} Team Cruz:{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/27766/the-ultimate-fighter-live-coach-dominick-cruz-no-longer-the-stinky-kid-in-class.mma |title='The Ultimate Fighter: Live' coach Dominick Cruz no longer the 'stinky kid in class' |first1=Dann |last1=Stupp |first2=John |last2=Morgan |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=March 8, 2012 |access-date=March 9, 2012}}\n*[[Dominick Cruz]], head coach\n*Doug Balzarini\n*[[Phil Davis (fighter)|Phil Davis]], wrestling coach\n*Eric DelFierro\n*[[Lloyd Irvin]]\n*[[Ross Pearson]]\n*[[Wilson Reis]]\n*[[Shanon Slack]]\n\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{color box|blue|border=silver}} Team Faber:{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/urijah-faber-reveals-coaching-staff-and-guests-for-ultimate-fighter-live-team |title=Urijah Faber Reveals Coaching Staff and Guests for Ultimate Fighter Live Team |first=Damon |last=Martin |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |date=March 7, 2012 |access-date=March 9, 2012}}\n*[[Urijah Faber]], head coach\n*Dustin Akbari\n*[[Justin Buchholz]]\n*Lance Palmer\n*Fabio Prado\n*Thonglor \"Master Thong\" Armatsena, Muay Thai coach\n*[[Danny Castillo]]\n*[[T.J. Dillashaw]]\n{{col-end}}\n\n===Fighters===\n;'''Team Cruz'''\n* [[Justin Lawrence (fighter)|Justin Lawrence]], [[Sam Sicilia]], [[Myles Jury]], [[Mike Rio]], [[James Vick]], [[Vinc Pichel]], Chris Tickle, [[Jeremy Larsen]]\n;'''Team Faber'''\n* [[Al Iaquinta]], [[Cristiano Marcello]], [[Daron Cruickshank]], [[Joe Proctor]], [[Michael Chiesa]], John Cofer, [[Andy Ogle]], [[Chris Saunders (mixed martial artist)|Chris Saunders]]\n\n; Fighters eliminated during the entry round\n*[[Akbarh Arreola]], Erin Beach, Jared Carlsten, [[Dakota Cochrane]], [[Drew Dober]], Mark Glover, Chase Hackett, [[James Krause (fighter)|James Krause]], Austin Lyons, Ali Maclean, [[Cody Pfister]], [[Jordan Rinaldi]], Jeff Smith, [[Jon Tuck]], Brendan Weafer, Johnavan Vistante\n\n==Episodes==\n;'''Episode 1 (March 9, 2012)'''\n* Two days prior to the live episode, the 32 fighters were introduced to Dana White and the two coaches, UFC bantamweight champion Dominick Cruz and Urijah Faber. White explains that an elimination round will be held and only 16 of the fighters will move on to the Ultimate Fighter house to compete during the rest of the season. He further explains that the elimination fights will each consist of a single five-minute round.\n* The bonus awards are explained:\n**Fan vote for best fight of the season earns each fighter $25,000.\n**Fan vote for best submission of the season earns the winner $25,000.\n**Fan vote for best knock out of the season earns the winner $25,000.\n**Any fight ending via submission, knock out, or referee stoppage earns the winner $5,000.\n**The winner of TUF 15 and his coach will also win a [[Harley Davidson]] motorcycle.\n**The winner of TUF 15 will earn a one-year sponsorship deal with Tapout.\n*The elimination round fights are held:{{cite web|url=http://boxing.nv.gov/2012%20Results%20Web/03-09-12%20TUF.pdf |title=Mixed martial arts results, Season 15 – The Ultimate Fighter |publisher=State of Nevada, Department of Business and Industry Athletic Commission |date=March 9, 2012 |access-date=March 25, 2012 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120617073556/http://boxing.nv.gov/2012%20Results%20Web/03-09-12%20TUF.pdf |archive-date=June 17, 2012 }}\n**'''Joe Proctor''' defeats Jordan Rinaldi by submission (guillotine choke) at 2:08\n**'''Cristiano Marcello''' defeats Jared Carlsten by submission (rear naked choke) at 2:43.\n**'''Sam Sicilia''' defeats Erin Beach by TKO (strikes) at :08.\n**'''Chris Tickle''' defeats Austin Lyons by TKO (strikes) at :24.\n**'''Andy Ogle''' defeats Brendan Weafer by unanimous decision (10–9, 10–9, 10–9).\n**'''Vinc Pichel''' defeats Cody Pfister by submission (rear naked choke) at 3:39.\n**'''John Cofer''' defeats Mark Glover by unanimous decision (10–9, 10–9, 10–9).\n**'''Chris Saunders''' defeats Chase Hackett by unanimous decision (10–9, 10–9, 10–9).\n**'''James Vick''' defeats Dakota Cochrane by split decision (9–10, 10–9, 10–9).\n**'''Michael Chiesa''' defeats Johnavan Vistante by submission (rear naked choke) at 2:05.\n**'''Mike Rio''' defeats Ali Maclean by submission (rear naked choke) at 3:32.\n**'''Justin Lawrence''' defeats James Krause by TKO (strikes) at 1:25.\n**'''Daron Cruickshank''' defeats Drew Dober by unanimous decision (10–9, 10–9, 10–9).\n**'''Jeremy Larsen''' defeats Jeff Smith by unanimous decision (10–9, 10–9, 10–9).\n**'''Al Iaquinta''' defeats Jon Tuck by unanimous decision (10–9, 10–9, 10–9).\n*Tuck appeared to break a right toe during the fight.\n**'''Myles Jury''' defeats Akbarh Arreola by unanimous decision (10–9, 10–9, 10–9).\n\n;'''Post Episode 1'''\nThe first episode earned a television rating consisting of 1.28 million viewers which was less than the 1.5 million viewers for [[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Bisping vs. Team Miller]].{{cite web |url=http://www.multichannel.com/article/481771-FX_Ultimate_Fighter_Pins_Nearly_1_3_Million_Viewers.php |title=FX 'Ultimate Fighter' Pins Nearly 1.3 Million Viewers |first=R. Thomas |last=Umstead |publisher=Multichannel News |date=March 13, 2012 |access-date=March 13, 2012}} Following his win in the elimination round, [[Jeremy Larsen]] was medically suspended by the [[Nevada Athletic Commission|Nevada State Athletic Commission]] (NSAC) due to a cut on his head. His suspension is scheduled to last until April 9. However, Keith Kizer, the NSAC Executive Director, said the NSAC and its doctors will evaluate Larsen each week to determine if Larsen can be medically cleared sooner.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/27826/tuf-live-cast-member-jeremy-larsen-medically-suspended-status-tbd.mma |title='TUF: Live' cast member Jeremy Larsen medically suspended, status TBD |first=Steven |last=Marrocco |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=March 13, 2012 |access-date=March 13, 2012}} Other medical suspensions from the elimination round fights include Jeff Smith ([[Arachnoid cyst]]), [[James Krause (fighter)|James Krause]] (possible ankle injury), Cody Pfister (cut to right eye), Austin Lyons (due to knock out) and Erin Beach (due to technical knock out and cut on lower lip).{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/ultimate-fighter-live-medical-suspensions-includes-one-fighter-who-made-the-house |title=Ultimate Fighter Live Medical Suspensions Includes One Fighter Who Made the House |first=Damon |last=Martin |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |date=March 13, 2012 |access-date=March 13, 2012}}\n\n; '''Episode 2: \"Embrace the War\" (March 16, 2012)'''\n* '''Saturday 8'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': The fighters and coaches are at the UFC training center with White. White announces that he will flip a coin (red for Cruz, blue for Faber) to pick a coach. The coach who wins the coin flip will have the option of picking the first fighter or the first fight. Faber wins the coin flip and decides to pick the first fight. Cruz makes the first fighter pick and the coaches alternate picking fighters for their teams.\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Coach\n!1st Pick\n!2nd Pick\n!3rd Pick\n!4th Pick\n!5th Pick\n!6th Pick\n!7th Pick\n!8th Pick\n|-\n!Cruz\n|Justin Lawrence\n|Sam Sicilia\n|Myles Jury\n|Mike Rio\n|James Vick\n|Vinc Pichel\n|Chris Tickle\n|Jeremy Larsen\n|-\n!Faber\n|Al Iaquinta\n|Cristiano Marcello\n|Daron Cruickshank\n|Joe Proctor\n|Michael Chiesa\n|John Cofer\n|Andy Ogle\n|Chris Saunders\n|}\n* '''Saturday 2'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''':Faber matches Vick against Cruickshank.\n**Before the fight announcement, Faber questions Cruz on a comment made in a UFC magazine interview where Cruz mentioned that Faber's family had probably helped him purchase his gym. Faber denies the statement and warns the champion to stay away from family comments. Cruz apologizes before the announcement.\n* '''Sunday 2'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': Michael Chiesa, at the training center, is told to call home and finds out that his father died the day before. Chiesa talks about his father having been ill and making him promise to stay on the show regardless of what happens. When Chiesa gets back to the TUF house, he tells Sam Sicilia, whom he knows from home, about his father's death.\n* '''Monday 9'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': White meets with Chiesa and says that he can go home for a couple days to be with his family to attend the funeral, and can then rejoin the show afterwards.\n* '''Thursday 4'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': The fighters weigh-in. Vick weighs in at {{convert|154|lb|kg}} and Cruickshank at {{convert|155.5|lb|kg}}.\n* '''Live''': '''James Vick''' defeats Daron Cruickshank by KO (knee) at 2:16 of the first round.\n**Following the fight, Cruz having won control is allowed to make the next fight pick. From his team, Cruz picks Lawrence, the first overall pick. Cruz then tells Faber that he can pick anyone from his team to face Lawrence. Faber hesitates before asking if anyone from his team wants to fight Lawrence. When no one volunteers, Faber tells Cruz to make the pick for him. Cruz picks Marcello to face Lawrence in a week's time.\n\n;'''Episode 3: \"Old School vs. New School\" (March 23, 2012)'''\n*'''Friday 8:15'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': Following the previous week's live episode there is discussion among both teams in regards to the hesitancy on the part of Team Faber to choose someone to face Justin Lawrence.\n*'''Sunday 8'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': Chris Tickle, wearing a gas mask, starts this season's prank war by attempting to move Faber's parking sign across the street. In attempting to do so, he breaks the sign and places it in a pile of tires used inside the UFC training center.\n*'''Monday 11'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': During the Team Faber training session, Marcello discusses strategy and prepares for his fight against Lawrence. In response to Tickle's prank, Team Faber puts tape on a large poster of Cruz making him appear like [[Eddie Munster]].\n*'''Tuesday 10:30'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': John Cofer makes a joke about how Tickle walks. Tickle gets offended by the joke and a verbal argument ensues.\n*'''Tuesday 11'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': During the Team Cruz training session, there is a focus for Lawrence on [[Brazilian jiu jitsu]] defense. The prank war also continues as the red team constructs a \"thong\" for the large poster of Faber, placing it on his \"butt chin.\"\n*'''Thursday 4'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': At the official weigh-ins, both Lawrence and Marcello weigh in at {{convert|156|lb|kg}}.\n*'''Live''': '''Justin Lawrence''' defeats Cristiano Marcello by KO (punches) at 3:15 of the second round.\n**Team Cruz maintains control of the fight pick. The fight for the following week is announced to be Myles Jury vs. Al Iaquinta, Team Faber's number 1 pick.\n\n;'''Episode 4: \"All the Pressure\" (March 30, 2012)'''\n*'''Saturday 9'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': During Team Cruz practice, Mike Rio tweaks a knee while sparring with Jury.\n*'''Sunday 3'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': Various fighters discuss the difficulties of being away from their families for three months without any contact while only being three weeks into the show.\n*'''Tuesday 2'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': Faber brings \"life coach\" Jim Peterson to TUF house to speak with all of the fighters. Chris Tickle decides not to attend the talk.\n*'''Wednesday 8'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': Rio is still complaining of pain in his knee.\n*'''Thursday 4'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': At the official weigh-ins, Jury weighs {{convert|154|lb|kg}} and Iaquinta weighed {{convert|155|lb|kg}}.\n*'''Live''': '''Al Iaquinta''' defeats Myles Jury by split decision after three rounds.\n**Team Faber gains control of the fight pick. The fight for the following week is announced to be Jeremy Larsen vs. Michael Chiesa.\n\n;'''Post Episode 4'''\n*The live fight between Al Iaquinta and Myles Jury went to a third round due to a draw following the first two rounds. Circumstances surrounding a network commercial break resulted in a three-minute break between the second and third rounds. Normally, there is only a one-minute break between rounds. White said in an interview following the episode that the longer breaks can result in fighters starting to cool down or cramp up. White stated that the UFC and FX will be working on ensuring that extended breaks between rounds does not occur again.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/28059/no-more-three-minute-commercial-breaks-on-tuf-live-white-says.mma |title=No more three-minute commercial breaks on 'TUF: Live,' White says |first1=Dann |last1=Stupp |first2=John |last2=Morgan |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=March 31, 2012 |access-date=April 6, 2012}}\n\n;'''Episode 5: \"That Miserable Feeling\" (April 6, 2012)'''\n*'''Friday''': Following the previous week's live fight and the announcement that Chiesa would be fighting Larsen, Sam Sicilia expresses hesitance to discuss Chiesa's fighting style. The two are friends and training partners back home and Siclia wants to remain that way and not betray Chiesa.\n*'''Saturday 9'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': Cruz talks to Sicilia and tries to find out about Chiesa's favorite submissions and fighting style. Sicilia tells Cruz he is uncomfortable talking about it.\n*'''Sunday 9'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': Chris Tickle has been complaining of pain in his toes. In talking with the UFC doctor, the symptoms resemble [[gout]]. The UFC doctor discusses the issue with Cruz and that tests will be performed to verify or eliminate gout as a possibility. Cruz is not convinced that the problem child is having any real problems and not Tickle disliking the training regimen. Tickle says it is not the training that he is having a problem with.\n*'''Monday 1:30'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': During training, Faber and Chiesa work on takedowns, grappling, and submissions.\n*'''Tuesday 3'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': During training, Tickle complains of stomach pains and Cruz tells him to work through them. In an interview, Cruz explains that he is getting tired of Tickle's excuses not to train.\n*'''Wednesday 1'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': Tickle visits the hospital to get the results of the gout tests performed. The tests have come back negative for gout.\n*'''Thursday 4'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': At the official weigh-ins, Larsen weighs {{convert|154|lb|kg}} and Chiesa weighs {{convert|155.5|lb|kg}}.\n*'''Live''': Late in the first round, Chiesa kneed Larsen in the head while Larsen had a knee touching the mat. The referee called for a timeout to allow Larsen to recover from the illegal blow and deducted a point from Chiesa.\n*'''Michael Chiesa''' defeats Jeremy Larsen by unanimous decision after two rounds.\n**Team Faber retains control of the fight pick. Faber announces the fight the following week will be between Tickle and Joe Proctor.\n\n;'''Episode 6: \"Dog with no Bark\" (April 13, 2012)'''\n*'''Friday''': It was discussed that Tickle was drunk during the previous week's fight. In the locker room, Tickle was laughing and making jokes about his just announced fight with Proctor. Cruz gets upset with Tickle's attitude and tells him to keep his mouth shut for the next week.\n*'''Saturday 10:30'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': Mike Rio complains of pains in one of his knees and having difficulties moving it. Rio and Lawrence have a discussion about the lifetime of a mixed martial arts fighter.\n**During practice, Cruz apologizes to Tickle for his comments the night before. The head coach explained that he was upset due to Jeremy Larsen's loss. Cruz says he wants Tickle to be himself.\n**Cruz pairs Justin Lawrence with Rio during practice and tells Lawrence not to throw spin kicks at Rio, although he does. Rio complains of the kicks feeling they were dangerous for practice. It causes Rio and Lawrence to become rougher with each other during the training session. To retaliate, Rio takes Lawrence down and submits him several times.\n*'''Saturday 5'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': While Team Faber is at practice, Tickle sets up a practical joke by placing a container of water on the front door to the Ultimate Fighter House. When Team Faber returns from practice, Proctor walks into the door and the container of water spills onto his head. Proctor laughs and believes it is a funny joke.\n*'''Sunday 11:30'''{{nbsp}}'''am''': The fighters discuss being away from their families and friends during [[Easter|Easter Sunday]]. Tickle cooks a turkey for the entire house and the fighters spend time outside with a makeshift [[Slip 'n Slide]].\n*'''Thursday 4'''{{nbsp}}'''pm''': At the official weigh-ins, Tickle weighs in at {{convert|153|lb|kg}} while eating a slice of pizza and Proctor weighs {{convert|155|lb|kg}}.\n*'''Live''': '''Joe Proctor''' defeats Chris Tickle by submission (rear naked choke) at 4:42 in the first round.\n**Team Faber retains control of the fight pick. Faber announces the fight for the following week to be John Cofer against Vinc Pichel.\n\n;'''Episode 7'''\n*'''Vinc Pichel''' defeats John Cofer by submission (arm triangle choke) at :44 in the third round.\n\n;'''Episode 8'''\n*'''Chris Saunders''' defeats Sam Sicilia by split decision after two rounds.\n\n;'''Episode 9'''\n* The Coaches' Challenge is presented by White and it is sponsored by the [[United States Marine Corps]].\n* The coaches are to climb a 20-foot high rope, shoot five targets with a handgun, flip tractor tires, shoot targets 50 and 70 feet away with an assault rifle, run carrying training dummies, shoot targets with a rifle again, run carrying 35 lbs of ammunition, and shoot a target with a grenade launcher.\n* Cruz starts off strong as he was faster to climb the rope, but his rival eventually catches up. Faber is quicker to shoot the target with the grenade launcher and wins the Coaches' Challenge.\n*'''Andy Ogle''' defeats Mike Rio by submission (rear naked choke) at 4:19 in the second round.\n\n;'''Episode 10'''\n*'''James Vick''' defeats Joe Proctor by unanimous decision after two rounds.\n*'''Michael Chiesa''' defeats Justin Lawrence by TKO (punches) at 1:02 in the third round.\n*In the last moments of the fight, Chiesa secured a full mount position on Lawrence.\n\n;'''Episode 11'''\n*'''Vinc Pichel''' defeats Chris Saunders by majority decision after two rounds.\n*'''Al Iaquinta''' defeats Andy Ogle by TKO (punches) at 4:44 in the first round.\n\n;'''Episode 12'''\n*'''Michael Chiesa''' defeats James Vick by TKO (strikes) at 1:55 in the second round.\n*'''Al Iaquinta''' defeats Vinc Pichel by unanimous decision after two rounds.\n*The finalists are now announced to be Chiesa and Iaquinta, both of Team Faber.\n\n===Episode list===\n{| class=\"wikitable plainrowheaders\"\n|-\n! style=\"width:3em\" | No.\n! It took place on June 1, 2012 at the [[Palms Casino Resort]] in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/TUF-Live-1-Finale#/fight|title=The Ultimate Fighter: Live Finale|work=UFC.com |date=April 9, 2012 |access-date=April 9, 2012}}\n\n===Background===\nFeatured were the finalists from ''The Ultimate Fighter: Live'' – Team Cruz vs Team Faber in the [[Lightweight (MMA)|Lightweight]] division.\n\nA rematch between [[Ian McCall (fighter)|Ian McCall]] and [[Demetrious Johnson (fighter)|Demetrious Johnson]] for the first round of the tournament to crown the first UFC Flyweight Champion was initially reported for this event.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/ian-mccall-vs-demetrious-johnson-2-scheduled-for-tuf-live-finale-june-1 |title=Ian McCall vs. Demetrious Johnson 2 Scheduled for TUF Live Finale June 1 |first=Damon |last=Martin |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |date=March 14, 2012 |access-date=March 14, 2012}} However, it was rescheduled for [[UFC on FX: Johnson vs. McCall]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-on-fx-3-fight-card-updates |title=UFC on FX 3 fight updates|publisher=ufc.com |date=March 20, 2012 |access-date=March 20, 2012}}\n\nByron Bloodworth was scheduled to face [[John Albert (fighter)|John Albert]] at the event. However, Bloodworth was forced out of the bout and replaced by promotional newcomer [[Erik Pérez]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/newcomer-erik-perez-steps-in-at-tuf-live-finale |title=Newcomer Erik Perez Steps In at TUF Live Finale |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |date=May 15, 2012 |access-date=May 15, 2012}}\n\n===Results===\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card Sports|date=June 2, 2012}}\n|gate=$2,200,000\n|buyrate=\n|purse=$1,501,000\n|previousevent= [[UFC on Fuel TV: Gustafsson vs. Silva]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC on Fox: Diaz vs. Miller]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 145: Jones vs. Evans''''' was a mixed martial arts event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]. It was the 9th UFC event of the year, and took place on April 21, 2012, at the [[Philips Arena]] in [[Atlanta, Georgia]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC145#/fight|title=UFC 145: Jones vs. Evans|publisher=ufc.com |access-date=2012-02-08}}{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.ca/event/UFC145/results|title=UFC 145 results|publisher=Ufc.com |date=April 21, 2012}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was initially set to take place on March 24, 2012, in [[Montreal]], Canada.{{cite web|url=https://bleacherreport.com/articles/992384-ufc-145-set-for-montreal-rory-macdonald-slated-to-face-che-mills|title=UFC 145 Set for Montreal, Rory MacDonald Slated to Face Che Mills |date=December 21, 2011|work=[[Bleacher Report]]|author=Joshua Carey}} However, in mid-January 2012, it was announced that the event was shelved due to inability to secure a championship fight, stemming from \"scheduling complications\".{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.co.uk/mma/story/_/id/7472376/ufc-145-set-march-montreal-postponed|title=UFC shelves show set for Montreal|date=January 17, 2012|work=[[ESPN]]}} In early February it was reported that the event would take place in [[Atlanta, Georgia]] on April 21.{{cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Jon-JonesRashad-Evans-Title-Headliner-Confirmed-for-UFC-145-40083|title=Jon Jones-Rashad Evans Title Headliner Confirmed for UFC 145|date=February 7, 2012|work=[[Sherdog]]|author=Mike Whitman}}\n\nThe headline fight at UFC 145 was a [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship]] contest between champion [[Jon Jones]] and challenger [[Rashad Evans]]. Jones and Evans are former teammates at [[Jackson's MMA]], under the tutelage of coach [[Greg Jackson (MMA trainer)|Greg Jackson]].{{cite web|url=https://lfpress.com/sports/othersports/2012/03/31/19575701.html|title=Bones Jones gets mentally tough for UFC 145|publisher=London Free Press|date=June 2, 2012|access-date=October 4, 2018|archive-date=August 20, 2012|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120820025145/http://www.lfpress.com/sports/othersports/2012/03/31/19575701.html|url-status=dead}} Evans was one of Jackson's most successful fighters, and was a core member of his training camp. Being in the same weightclass, and a long-time member of Jackson's MMA, the UFC veteran Evans naturally took the young and inexperienced fighter Jones under his wing. In the months leading to UFC 145, the two engaged in a bitter and public feud.{{cite web|url=https://www.latimes.com/sports/la-xpm-2012-apr-15-la-sp-pugmire-qa-20120416-story.html|title=Q&A: Jon Jones says he ignores UFC foe Rashad Evans' head games|work=Los Angeles Times|date=June 2, 2012}}\n\nAt [[UFC 128]] (March 19, 2011), Evans was expected to fight for the Light Heavyweight title against then-champion [[Mauricio Rua]]. However, a knee injury forced Evans off of the card. Needing an opponent for Rua at UFC 128, the UFC offered Jones the match. Jones accepted, with Evans' blessing. Shortly afterwards, Jones stated in an interview with MMA reporter [[Ariel Helwani]] he would be willing to fight Evans if the UFC asked. Evans felt betrayed by his teammate and friend, starting a feud which forced Evans to leave Jackson's MMA. Evans then joined the Imperial Athletics camp in Florida.{{cite web|url=https://www.usatoday.com/sports/mma/post/2012-04-09/evans-perceives-improved-more-confident-jon-jones/667815/1|title=Evans perceives improved, more confident Jon Jones|publisher=[[USA Today]]|date=June 2, 2012}}\n\nSince UFC 128, Jones had defended his championship twice against former champions ([[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]] and [[Lyoto Machida]]). Evans had defeated former champion [[Tito Ortiz]], as well as [[Phil_Davis_(fighter)|Phil Davis]], to setting the stage for \"the most anticipated Light Heavyweight bout since [[Chuck Liddell]] fought [[Tito Ortiz]]\".{{cite web|url=http://www.ibtimes.com/sportsnet/ufc/feud-fight-jon-jones-vs-rashad-evans_67.htm|title=Jon Jones vs. Rashad Evans UFC 145: The Feud and The Fight|work=International Business Times|date=June 2, 2012}}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }} This was the third scheduled contest between the two. The first match was scrapped due to a hand injury to Jones, the second because of a thumb injury to Evans.{{cite web|url=http://www.nypost.com/p/sports/more_sports/with_bones_jones_talks_about_evans_di0fuYbEbGpAEZ7g8OJ3CP|title=Jon 'Bones' Jones talks about new video game, title bout with Evans|publisher=New York Post|date=June 2, 2012}}\n\n[[UFC Primetime]] returned to promote the main event.{{cite web|url=http://www.newsday.com/sports/mixed-martial-arts/fightin-words-1.811935/ufc-primetime-re-shaping-rashad-evans-1.3654042|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120414053038/http://www.newsday.com/sports/mixed-martial-arts/fightin-words-1.811935/ufc-primetime-re-shaping-rashad-evans-1.3654042|url-status=dead|archive-date=April 14, 2012|title=UFC Primetime: Re-shaping Rashad Evans|publisher=Long Island Newsday|date=June 2, 2012}}\n\n[[Matt Wiman]] was expected to face [[Mark Bocek]] at this event, but was forced off the card with an injury and replaced by the returning [[John Alessio]].{{cite web|url=https://lfpress.com/sports/othersports/2012/04/04/19595316.html|title=Vancouver's Alessio returns to UFC|publisher=London Free Press|date=June 2, 2012}}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }}\n\n[[John Makdessi]] weighed in two pounds over the Lightweight limit. He was fined 20 percent of his purse, and his fight with [[Anthony Njokuani]] became a [[catchweight]] bout at 158 lb.{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/7837692/jon-jones-rashad-evans-make-weight-ufc-main-event|title=Fighters make weight for UFC 145|publisher=[[ESPN]]|date=June 2, 2012}}\n\n==Event==\n[[File:Phillips Arena after the 14 March 2008 Downtown Atlanta storm.JPG|right|thumb|250px|15,545 fans filled [[Philips Arena]] to witness ''UFC 145'' and the grudge match between Jones and Evans.]]\nThe event consisted of twelve mixed martial arts bouts, sanctioned by the Georgia Athletic and Entertainment Commission and contested under the [[Unified Rules of Mixed Martial Arts]]. All bouts were scheduled for three five-minute rounds except for the main event championship match between Jones and Evans, which was five five-minute rounds. Two of the preliminary fights were streamed on [[Facebook]], and four were televised on [[FX (TV Channel)|FX]]. Attendance was 15,545, with a gate of $2,200,000. The pay-per-view buyrate was 700,000.\n\n===Preliminary card===\nAll but one of the event's six preliminary fights went the full three rounds, ending in a decision.\n\nThe first bout of the evening was between [[Maximo Blanco]] and [[Marcus Brimage]]. After three uneventful rounds between the hesitant fighters, the judges scored the contest 29–28 Blanco, 30–27 Brimage, and 29–28 for the winner by split decision, Marcus Brimage. Immediately after the fight concluded, Blanco executed a backflip, which was mimicked by Brimage. The two fighters then engaged in an impromptu backflip competition to the delight of the fans.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/UFC-145-Jones-vs-Evans-Live-Results-and-PlaybyPlay-41757|title=UFC 145 Jones vs. Evans' Live Results and Play-By-Play|publisher=[[Sherdog]]|date=June 2, 2012}}\n\nIn the second match, [[Keith Wisniewski]] took on UFC newcomer [[Chris Clements (fighter)|Chris Clements]]. In the first round, Wisniewski clinched and dragged Clements to the mat, and passed to full mount. Clements regained half guard as Wisniewski landed hard shots. With two minutes left in the round, referee Fernando Yamasaki issued a controversial stand-up order. Following the stand-up, Clements dropped Wisniewski with a turning kick to the body. Wisniewski recovered and the two traded punches shortly before the round ended. After a close second round, Clements controlled the third, landing powerful right hand punches and dropping Wisniewski with a knee. He closed out the fight with strong [[ground-and-pound]] as time expired, sending the fight to the judges for a decision. The judges scored the bout 29–28 Clements, 29–28 Wisniewski, and 30–27 for the winner by split decision, Chris Clements.\n\nIn the third bout, [[Mac Danzig]] fought [[Efraín Escudero]]. In the first round, Escudero unsuccessfully attempted a [[Heel hooks|heel hook]] submission. Danzig proceeded to [[Back mount|take Escudero's back]] and attempt a [[rear-naked choke]] in one motion, but Escudero escaped. After three close rounds, the bout went to a decision. The judges scored it 30–27, 30–27, and 29–28 for Mac Danzig, the winner by unanimous decision.\n\nThe fourth match pitted [[Anthony Njokuani]] against [[taekwondo]] fighter [[John Makdessi]]. The first round was defined by kicks. While Makdessi was the aggressor, Njokuani landed more kicks. In the second round, Njokuani continued to use his reach advantage to land kicks and punches. Makdessi could not get his timing down and Njokuani continued to counter. The third round was more of the same until the horn sounded, and all three judges scored the bout 30–27 for the winner by unanimous decision, Anthony Njokuani.\n\nIn the fifth fight, [[Stephen Thompson (fighter)|Stephen Thompson]] faced [[Matt Brown (fighter)|Matt Brown]]. In the first two rounds, Thompson had success striking on the feet but had trouble stopping Brown's takedowns. Thompson started the second round strong, wobbling Brown with kicks and punches. Brown then knocked Thompson down with an elbow and, from the top, landed elbows and punches which opened a cut on Thompson's face. Brown dominated the third round by securing a mounted crucifix and then a mounted triangle. Brown finished out the fight by pummeling a bloody Thompson until the horn sounded. The judges scored the bout 30–27, 29–27, and 30–27 for Matt Brown, the winner by unanimous decision.\n\nIn the sixth bout of the evening, heavyweights [[Travis Browne]] and [[Chad Griggs]] squared off. In the first round, Browne secured a takedown and landed in [[half guard]]. Browne easily transitioned into the [[full mount|full mount position]] and quickly secured an [[arm triangle choke]]. Griggs [[submission (combat sport)|tapped out]] at 2:29 of the first round, in the first finish of the night. The UFC awarded Browne with the \"Submission of the Night\" bonus award for his performance.\n\n===Main card===\n[[File:UFC 74 Respect Bout.jpg|left|thumb|250px|Shot of The Octagon from UFC 74: Clay Guida vs. Marcus Aurelio]]\nThe first bout on the main card was a lightweight match between [[Mark Bocek]] and [[John Alessio]]. Bocek started strong and dominated the first round after a takedown. From there, he landed several big elbows. He continued to grapple and soften up Alessio with punches until the end of the round. Both men slowed down in the second round, but Bocek controlled the action. In the final round, Bocek transitioned to full back mount from a [[Body Triangle (grappling)|body triangle]] on Alessio. However, Bocek failed to fully apply a rear-naked choke, and Alessio escaped. Neither fighter could finish the fight before time ended, and the fight went to a decision. The three judges scored the bout 30–27, 29–28, and 30–27 for Mark Bocek, the winner by unanimous decision.\n\nThe next fight was between two featherweight fighters, [[Mark Hominick]] and Eddie Yagin. Hominick survived the first round after being knocked down by a right [[uppercut]]/left [[Hook (boxing)|hook]] combination from Yagin. At the start of the second round, Hominick looked less tired than Yagin. Hominick landed solid punches until Yagin landed another big shot of his own. Yagin followed up with hard shots on the ground but, once again, Hominick recovered. Hominick peppered Yagin in the third round with hooks and [[jab]]s, while Yagin threw power shots. Hominick continued to land punches throughout the round, and both fighter's faces were bruised and bloody by the end. The fight ended in a split decision. The first judge scored the bout 29–28 for Yagin. The second scored it 29–28 for Hominick. The third scored it 29–28 in favor of the winner, Eddie Yagin. This fight earned both men $65,000 \"Fight of the Night\" bonus awards.\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light Heavyweight\n|[[Jon Jones]] [[UFC 144|UFC 144: Edgar vs. Henderson]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC on Fuel TV: Gustafsson vs. Silva]]\n}}\n'''''UFC on FX: Alves vs. Kampmann''''' (also known as '''''UFC on FX 2''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held live by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on Saturday March 3, 2012 at the [[Allphones Arena]] in [[Sydney]], Australia.{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFCFX2#/fight|title=UFC on FX 2: Alves vs. Kampmann|work=UFC.com|date=December 16, 2011}}\n\n==Background==\nUFC on FX: Alves vs. Kampmann was the promotion's 200th event.\n\nThe event was the third [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] card to be held in [[Australia]] and the second to be televised on [[FX (TV channel)|FX]]. Ticket sales for the event were slower than the two previous [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] events held in [[Australia]], [[UFC 110]] and [[UFC 127]]. These events led to record ticket-sales, with [[UFC 110]] becoming the second fastest sell-out in [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] history{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/3/1/1331707/sportsbusiness-journal-ufc-sets|title=SportsBusiness Journal: UFC Sets Company Merchandise Sales Record in Australia|publisher=BloodyElbow.com|date=March 1, 2010|access-date=January 18, 2012|archive-date=March 3, 2011|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110303192213/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2010/3/1/1331707/sportsbusiness-journal-ufc-sets|url-status=dead}} and [[UFC 127]] equaled the fastest sell-out (30 minutes).{{Cite web|url=http://www.theroar.com.au/2011/12/15/ufc-returns-to-australia-with-alves-vs-kampmann/|title = UFC returns to Australia with Alves vs Kampmann}} UFC on FX: Alves vs. Kampmann was the most successful \"Fight Night\" event in the company's history in terms of box office.\n\nThe event also made history by playing host the first ever [[Flyweight (MMA)|flyweight]] fights in [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] history as part of the semifinals for the [[List of UFC champions#Tournament winners|Flyweight Tournament]], the winner of which would be crowned the inaugural [[List of UFC champions#Flyweight Championship|UFC Flyweight Champion]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-flyweights-added-ufc-on-fx-3|title=The Flyweights Are Here|work=UFC.com|date=December 11, 2011|access-date=December 11, 2011}}{{Dead link|date=July 2018 |bot=InternetArchiveBot |fix-attempted=no }}{{cite web |url=https://www.usatoday.com/sports/mma/story/2012-02-29/UFC-flyweight-tournament/53308474/1|title=Flyweight division makes UFC debut in Australia| work=usatoday.com |date=March 2, 2012 |access-date=May 14, 2012}}\n\n[[Jared Hamman]] was expected to face [[Kyle Noke]] at this event, but withdrew from the bout due to an injury. Noke instead faced promotional newcomer [[Andrew Craig]].{{cite web |url=http://mmaweekly.com/jared-hamman-hurt-kyle-noke-gets-new-opponent-for-ufc-on-fx-2|title=Jared Hamman Hurt, Kyle Noke Gets New Opponent for UFC on FX 2 |work=mmaweekly.com |date=January 19, 2012}}\n\n[[Robbie Peralta]] was expected to rematch [[Mackens Semerzier]] at this event, but pulled out of the bout due to an injury. Semerzier instead faced [[Daniel Pineda (fighter)|Daniel Pineda]].{{cite web |url=http://mmaweekly.com/daniel-pineda-repairs-ufc-on-fx-2-fight-card|title=Daniel Pineda Repairs UFC on FX 2 Fight Card |work=mmaweekly.com |date=February 6, 2012}}\n\n==Scoring controversy==\nA stipulation put in place for the flyweight tournament stated that if any of the tournament fights were to end in a draw, a fourth \"[[Sudden death (sport)|sudden death]]\" round would take place. The [[Demetrious Johnson]] and [[Ian McCall (fighter)|Ian McCall]] fight went the distance and the Australian Athletic Commission collected and totaled the judges' score cards. The fight was announced as a majority decision win for Johnson. However, upon reviewing the point totals later in the evening, the athletic commission discovered that they had added up the judges' score cards incorrectly and that the fight should have been declared a majority draw and resulted in a fourth round. The athletic commission informed UFC officials of the error and at the post-fight press conference [[Dana White]] announced that both fighters were made aware of the situation, that the Flyweight Tournament fight between Johnson and McCall had been scored incorrectly, and that fight did indeed end in a majority draw. The error was corrected and the majority decision win for Johnson was overturned, but the previously announced fourth round tie breaker could not be fought. Johnson and McCall would be forced to repeat their fight, thus delaying the finish of the first round of the [[List of UFC champions#Tournament winners|Flyweight Tournament]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/27686/ufc-on-fx-2s-johnson-mccall-actually-a-draw-tabulation-error-leads-to-rematch|title=UFC on FX 2's Johnson-McCall actually a draw, tabulation error leads to rematch|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|date=March 3, 2012}}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }} Johnson and McCall would face each other again three months later at [[UFC on FX 3]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/27911/johnson-mccall-rematch-pague-papazian-slated-for-ufc-on-fx-3 |title=Johnson-McCall rematch slated for UFC on FX 3 |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=March 20, 2012 |access-date=March 20, 2012 }}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (FX)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Martin 2012}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=UFC 146: dos Santos vs. Mir\n|image=UFC-146-Dos-Santos-Mir-poster.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date=May 26, 2012\n|venue=[[MGM Grand Garden PPV Buys |work=bloodyelbow.com |date=June 6, 2012 |access-date=June 10, 2012 |archive-date=June 9, 2012 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120609002624/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2012/6/6/3068877/ufc-146-500000-ppv-buys-ufc-on-fx-ratings-decline-mma-news |url-status=dead }}{{cite web |url=http://mmafrenzy.com/28917/ufc-146-final-live-gate-and-ppv-buyrates |title= UFC 146 Final Live Gate and PPV Buyrates |work=mmafrenzy.com}}\n|purse=$1,513,000\n|previousevent=[[2012 in UFC#UFC on Fuel TV: The Korean Zombie vs. Poirier|UFC on Fuel TV: The Korean Zombie vs. Poirier]]\n|followingevent=[[The Ultimate Fighter: Live#The Ultimate Fighter 15 Finale|The Ultimate Fighter: Live Finale]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 146: dos Santos vs. Mir''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] (MMA) [[pay-per-view]] event (PPV) promoted by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC) on May 26, 2012, at the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]] in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]]. The main card featured five MMA bouts which were shown on PPV, as well as seven preliminary bouts which were shown on [[FX (TV channel)|FX]], and [[Facebook]]. This was the first time in UFC history that the main card featured all heavyweight bouts. It was the twelfth UFC event of 2012.\n\nThe main event featured the [[UFC Heavyweight Championship|UFC Heavyweight Champion]], [[Junior dos Santos]], defending his title against former two-time UFC Heavyweight Champion [[Frank Mir]]. The contest was heavily promoted, featuring the return of [[UFC Primetime]] to promote the UFC Heavyweight Championship. The co-main event of the evening was a battle between former heavyweight champion [[Cain Velasquez]], and newly acquired [[Strikeforce (mixed martial arts)|Strikeforce]] heavyweight, [[Antônio Silva (fighter)|Antônio Silva]], who made his UFC debut.{{cite news |url=http://latino.foxnews.com/latino/sports/2012/05/27/ufc-146-results-junior-dos-santos-takes-down-frank-mir/|title=UFC 146 Results: Junior dos Santos Takes Down Frank Mir|work=Fox News |date=May 27, 2012 |access-date=May 26, 2012}}\n\n==Background==\n[[Frank Mir]] was riding a three fight win streak leading up to this event, including a submission victory over [[Antônio Rodrigo Nogueira]], a friend and mentor of [[Junior dos Santos]], at [[UFC 140]]. Nogueira refused to tap to the [[kimura lock|kimura]] lock submission attempt, until it broke after his arm snapped under the pressure, then he tapped. Not only was Mir the first fighter to ever submit Nogueira, but he did so in devastating fashion. This was the first title defense for dos Santos since winning the belt by knocking out [[Cain Velasquez]] in November 2011 at [[UFC on Fox: Velasquez vs. Dos Santos]].{{cite news|url=http://www.latimes.com/sports/la-sp-ufc-dos-santos-mir-20120526,0,5401570.story |title=Junior Dos Santos out to avoid bad break against Frank Mir in UFC |work=LA Daily News |date=May 25, 2012 |access-date=May 25, 2012 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120526093722/http://www.latimes.com/sports/la-sp-ufc-dos-santos-mir-20120526,0,5401570.story |archive-date=May 26, 2012 }}\n\n[[UFC Primetime]] returned to promote the [[UFC Heavyweight Championship]] between Mir and dos Santos.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/27784/ufc-primetime-returns-to-fx-for-ufc-146-dos-santos-vs-overeem.mma|title='UFC Primetime' airs on FX for 'UFC 146: Dos Santos vs. Overeem'|work=mmajunkie.com|date=March 10, 2012|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120728121920/http://mmajunkie.com/news/27784/ufc-primetime-returns-to-fx-for-ufc-146-dos-santos-vs-overeem.mma|archive-date=July 28, 2012}} The heavyweight championship at UFC 146 was the featured fight on the main card, which for the first time in UFC history had all heavyweight bouts.{{cite web|url=http://espn.go.com/extra/mma/topics/_/page/ufc-146|archive-url=https://archive.today/20120708125505/http://espn.go.com/extra/mma/topics/_/page/ufc-146|url-status=dead|archive-date=July 8, 2012|title=UFC 146 Dos Santos vs. Mir|work=ESPN|date=April 24, 2012|access-date=May 14, 2012}}\n\n[[Evan Dunham]] was expected to face [[Edson Barboza]] at the event. However, Dunham was forced from the event with an injury and replaced by returning veteran [[Jamie Varner]].{{cite web|url=http://www.azcentral.com/sports/azetc/articles/2012/05/18/20120518whats-up-phoenix-mma-fighter-jamie-varner.html|title=What's up with Phoenix MMA fighter Jamie Varner|work=azcentral.com|date=May 22, 2012|access-date=May 27, 2012}}\n\n[[Mark Hunt]] was originally scheduled to face [[Stefan Struve]] on the main card. However, on May 17, 2012, Hunt withdrew from the bout due to injury and was replaced by [[Lavar Johnson]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/ufc/2012/5/17/3027315/mark-hunt-out-of-ufc-146-lavar-johnson-now-meets-stefan-struve|title=Mark Hunt Out of UFC 146; Lavar Johnson Now Meets Stefan Struve |work=mmafighting.com |date=May 17, 2012 |access-date=May 17, 2012}}\n\n===Alistair Overeem removal and card changes===\nOriginally scheduled to headline the event was a Heavyweight Championship bout between one of UFC's newest stars, [[Alistair Overeem]] (who defeated former champion [[Brock Lesnar]] in his UFC debut to earn the title shot), and champion [[Junior dos Santos]]. On April 4, 2012, however, the [[Nevada State Athletic Commission]] (NSAC) announced Overeem had failed his pre-fight drug test.{{cite news|url=https://www.usatoday.com/sports/mma/story/2012-05-25/mma-wrestles-with-testosterone-testing/55197858/1|title=UFC, MMA wrestling with testosterone issues|publisher=USA Today |access-date=May 27, 2012|date=May 25, 2012}} Overeem's March drug test showed a 14:1 [[Epitestosterone|testosterone-epitestosterone ratio]], according to the NSAC.\n\nThis resulted in a considerable shake up of the matches on the main card.{{cite web|url=https://vancouversun.com/sports/scrambles+down+main+events+major+views/6505313/story.html|title=UFC scrambles to pin down main events for major pay-per-views|work=Vancouver Sun|date=April 23, 2012|access-date=April 25, 2012}}{{dead link|date=April 2013}} On April 20, 2012, UFC President [[Dana White]] announced that Junior dos Santos would now defend his title against [[Frank Mir]] instead.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/28352/alistair-overeem-out-frank-mir-in-against-junior-dos-santos-at-ufc-146.mma|title=Alistair Overeem out, Frank Mir in against Junior Dos Santos at UFC 146|work=mmajunkie.com|date=April 20, 2012|access-date=April 20, 2012|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120423161508/http://mmajunkie.com/news/28352/alistair-overeem-out-frank-mir-in-against-junior-dos-santos-at-ufc-146.mma|archive-date=April 23, 2012}} Two days later, White announced [[Cain Velasquez]] (who was originally slated to fight Mir in the co-main event) would face [[Antônio Silva (fighter)|Antônio Silva]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2012/4/22/2967443/ufc-146-cain-velasquez-vs-antonio-silva-booked-for-may-26-in-las-vegas|title=UFC 146: Cain Velasquez vs Antonio Silva booked for May 26 in Las Vegas|work=mmamania.com|date=April 22, 2012|access-date=April 22, 2012}} [[Roy Nelson (fighter)|Roy Nelson]] (who was originally scheduled to fight Silva, and then briefly [[Gabriel Gonzaga]]){{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/28398/gabriel-gonzaga-fills-open-slot-against-roy-nelson-at-ufc-146.mma|title=Gabriel Gonzaga fills open slot against Roy Nelson at UFC 2012}} faced [[Dave Herman (fighter)|Dave Herman]].{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/7881102/roy-nelson-fight-dave-herman-ufc-146-gabriel-gonzaga-withdraws|title=Roy Nelson to fight Nelson vs Dave Herman booked with Gabriel Gonzaga injured |work=mmamania.com|date=May 2, 2012|access-date=May 2, 2012}} And [[Shane del Rosario]] (who was originally slated to fight Gonzaga) faced [[Stipe Miocic]].{{cite web|url=http://www.lasvegassun.com/news/2012/may/24/former-college-athlete-current-firefighter-stipe-m/|title=Firefighter, former college athlete Stipe Miocic making his mark in the UFC|work=Las Vegas Sun|date=May 24, 2012|access-date=May 27, 2012}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Junior dos Santos]] (also known as '''''UFC on Fuel TV 2''''' or '''''UFC: Sweden''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on April 14, 2012, at the [[Ericsson Globe|Ericsson Globe Arena]] in [[Stockholm]], [[Sweden]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFCSweden#/fight|title=UFC on Fuel TV: Gustafsson vs. Silva|publisher=ufc.com |date=January 11, 2012}}\n\n==Background==\nThis was the first [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] event held in [[Sweden]],{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/26810/ufc-on-fuel-tv-2-confirmed-for-april-14-in-stockholm-sweden.mma|title=UFC on FUEL TV 2 confirmed for April 14 in Stockholm, Sweden|publisher=mmajunkie.com |date=January 4, 2012}} and was the fastest selling European event in [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] history, selling out in three hours.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/26978/ufc-on-fuel-tv-2-in-sweden-sells-out-in-three-hours.mma|title=UFC on FUEL TV 2 in Sweden sells out in three hours|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=January 14, 2012|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120116215455/http://mmajunkie.com/news/26978/ufc-on-fuel-tv-2-in-sweden-sells-out-in-three-hours.mma|archive-date=January 16, 2012}}\n\nThe preliminary card for this event streamed live on [[Facebook]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/28119/ufc-on-fuel-tv-2-prelims-slated-for-facebook-with-1230-p-m-et-start-time.mma|title=UFC on FUEL TV 2 prelims slated for Facebook with 12:30 p.m. ET start time|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=April 5, 2012|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120407013053/http://mmajunkie.com/news/28119/ufc-on-fuel-tv-2-prelims-slated-for-facebook-with-1230-p-m-et-start-time.mma|archive-date=April 7, 2012}}\n\nA rematch between [[Ross Pearson]] and [[Dennis Siver]] at [[Featherweight (MMA)|featherweight]] was briefly linked to this event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/26945/ross-pearson-vs-dennis-siver-ii-not-happening-at-ufc-on-fuel-tv-2.mma|title=Ross Pearson vs. Dennis Siver II not happening at UFC on FUEL TV 2|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=January 12, 2012|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120115022902/http://mmajunkie.com/news/26945/ross-pearson-vs-dennis-siver-ii-not-happening-at-ufc-on-fuel-tv-2.mma|archive-date=January 15, 2012}} However, Siver instead faced [[Diego Nunes (fighter)|Diego Nunes]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/27113/diego-nunes-vs-dennis-siver-added-to-ufc-on-fuel-tv-2.mma|title=Diego Nunes vs. Dennis Siver added to UFC on FUEL TV 2|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=January 24, 2012}}\n\n[[Mike Goldberg]] did play by play for this event in place of [[Jon Anik]] due to scheduling issues.{{cite web|url=http://mmanytt.se/ufc/mike-goldberg-ersatter-jon-anik-som-kommentator-pa-ufc-sweden/|title=Mike Goldberg ersätter Jon Anik som kommentator på UFC: Sweden|publisher=mmanytt.se |date=January 29, |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC147#/fight|title=UFC® 147 Live on Pay-Per-View: Silva vs. Franklin II|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|access-date=May 9, 2012}}\n\n==Background==\n\nThe finals in the featherweight and middleweight tournaments of ''[[The Ultimate Fighter: Brazil]]'' were held on this card.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/26936/tuf-brazil-features-featherweights-and-middleweights-tourney-finals-on-ppv.mma |title=TUF Brazil features featherweights and middleweights, tourney finals on PPV |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-01-14 |last1=Morgan |last2=Stupp |first1=John |first2=Dann |author-link1=John Morgan (martial artist) |author-link2=Dann Stupp |access-date=2012-02-13 |archive-date=2012-07-12 |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120712103714/http://mmajunkie.com/news/26936/tuf-brazil-features-featherweights-and-middleweights-tourney-finals-on-ppv.mma |url-status=dead }} [[Daniel Sarafian]] was originally scheduled to compete in the Middleweight finals, but pulled out due to injury. He was replaced by semifinalist [[Sérgio Moraes]].{{cite web |url=http://m.ufc.com/news/ufc-147-fight-card-updates|title=Sarafian Injured, Out of TUF Brasil Finale at UFC 147|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|date=June 18, 2012}} [[Renee Forte]], a Middleweight for Team Wanderlei, was scheduled to fight Moraes, but was removed from the card when Moraes was enlisted to fill in for Sarafian.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/news/New-TUF-Brazil-Middleweight-Final-0618|title=NEW THE ULTIMATE FIGHTER BRAZIL® MIDDLEWEIGHT FINAL ANNOUNCED FOR UFC® 147 |publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship |date=June 18, 2012 |access-date=June 20, 2012}} UFC Officials have promised Forte a fight on a future UFC card.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/29237/despite-no-ufc-147-slot-tuf-brazil-member-renee-forte-will-get-future-ufc-fight.mma |title=Despite no UFC 147 slot, 'TUF: Brazil' member Renee Forte will get future UFC fight |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=June 18, 2012 |access-date=June 20, 2012 |author=Staff |archive-date=June 20, 2012 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120620100652/http://mmajunkie.com/news/29237/despite-no-ufc-147-slot-tuf-brazil-member-renee-forte-will-get-future-ufc-fight.mma |url-status=dead }}\n\nThis event also saw other significant changes to its original card. The expected rematch between [[Anderson Silva]] and [[Chael Sonnen]] was moved to [[UFC 148]] on July 7, 2012 in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]] and the expected co-feature of the Brazilian event, a rematch between [[Vitor Belfort]] and [[Wanderlei Silva]] was to headline the event.{{cite web |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/UFC-to-Hold-Presser-in-Rio-on-Tuesday-Source-Says-Spider-Defense-Likely-Moving-to-Las-Vegas-42031 |title=UFC to Hold Presser in Rio on Tuesday; Source Says 'Spider' Defense Likely Moving to Las Vegas|first=Mike |last=Whitman |publisher=Sherdog.com |date=April 20, 2012 |access-date=April 20, 2012}} On April 24, the changes regarding Silva/Sonnen were confirmed.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/28404/anderson-silva-vs-chael-sonnen-title-fight-moved-from-ufc-147-to-ufc-148.mma |title=Anderson Silva vs. Chael Sonnen title fight moved from UFC 147 to UFC 148 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=April 24, 2012 |access-date=April 24, 2012 |last=Stupp |first=Dann |archive-date=April 26, 2012 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120426211813/http://mmajunkie.com/news/28404/anderson-silva-vs-chael-sonnen-title-fight-moved-from-ufc-147-to-ufc-148.mma |url-status=dead }} Plans were then being made for Featherweight champion [[José Aldo]], who was scheduled to defend his title at [[UFC 149: Aldo vs. Koch|UFC 149]], to headline this event. However, it was reported April 28, 2012, that Aldo would remain on the [[UFC 149: Aldo vs. Koch|UFC 149]] card and defend his title against [[Erik Koch]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/28462/champ-jose-aldo-vs-erik-koch-title-fight-headlines-ufc-149-in-calgary.mma |title=Champ Jose Aldo vs. Erik Koch title fight headlines UFC 149 in Calgary |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-04-28 |access-date=2012-09-16 |last1=Morgan |last2=Stupp |first1=John |first2=Dann |author-link1=John Morgan (martial artist) |author-link2=Dann Stupp |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120712025314/http://mmajunkie.com/news/28462/champ-jose-aldo-vs-erik-koch-title-fight-headlines-ufc-149-in-calgary.mma |archive-date=2012-07-12 }}\n\nOn May 26, [[Vitor Belfort]] was forced to pull out of the card after breaking his left hand while training.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/28926/vitor-belfort-pulled-from-ufc-147-main-event-undergoes-surgery-for-broken-hand.mma |title=Vitor Belfort pulled from UFC 147 main event, undergoes surgery for broken hand |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=May 27, 2012 |access-date=May 27, 2012 |last=Morgan |first=John |archive-date=May 29, 2012 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120529124549/http://mmajunkie.com/news/28926/vitor-belfort-pulled-from-ufc-147-main-event-undergoes-surgery-for-broken-hand.mma |url-status=dead }} Silva's opponent was changed to [[Rich Franklin]] in a 190 lb catchweight bout.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/28961/rich-franklin-steps-into-ufc-147-headliner-against-wanderlei-silva.mma |title=Rich Franklin steps into UFC 147 headliner against Wanderlei Silva |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=May 30, 2012 |access-date=May 30, 2012 |last=Stupp |first=Dann |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20120712000532/http://mmajunkie.com/news/28961/rich-franklin-steps-into-ufc-147-headliner-against-wanderlei-silva.mma |archive-date=July 12, 2012 }} Franklin and Silva faced each other previously in June 2009 at [[UFC 99]], where Franklin defeated Silva via unanimous decision in a bout that earned both participants ''Fight of the Night'' honors.\n\nDue to the multitude of changes to the main card, the UFC offered a full refund for any ticket-holder no longer wanting to see the event live. This is the first time in the promotion's history that a full refund for an event has been offered before the event took place,{{cite web |url=http://www.cagepotato.com/sign-of-the-apocalypse-103-the-ufc-is-offering-full-refunds-for-ufc-147/|publisher=CagePotato.com|title=Sign of the Apocalypse #103: The UFC is Offering Full Refunds for UFC 147 |date=June 20, 2012 |access-date=June 20, 2012}} and the window for receiving the refund was only three days: 18 June 2012 to 20 June 2012. \n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Catchweight (190 lb)\n|[[Rich Franklin]]\n|def.\n|[[Wanderlei Silva]]\n| Decision (unanimous) (49–46, 49–46, 49–46)\n| 5\n| 5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Cezar Ferreira]]\n|def.\n|[[Sérgio Moraes]]\n| Decision (unanimous) (29–28, 30–27, 30–27)\n| 3\n| 5:00\n|TUF Brazil Middleweight Tournament final bout. \n|buyrate=\n|previousevent=[[UFC 151|UFC 151: Jones vs. Henderson]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC on Fuel TV: Struve vs. Miocic]]\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]}}\n'''''UFC 152: Jones vs. Belfort ''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] [[pay-per-view]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on September 22, 2012, at the [[Air Canada Centre]] in [[Toronto]], Ontario, Canada.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC152 |title=UFC 152: Jones vs. Belfort|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|access-date=2012-08-23}}\n\n==Background==\n[[Rory MacDonald (fighter)|Rory MacDonald]] was expected to face former dual-division champion [[B.J. Penn]] at the event, but MacDonald pulled out of the bout after sustaining a cut to the forehead while training.{{cite web |url=https://bleacherreport.com/articles/1285425-ufc-152-rory-macdonald-injured-and-out-of-bj-penn-fight |title=UFC 152: Rory MacDonald Injured and out of B.J. Penn Fight |publisher=[[Bleacher Report]] |date=2012-08-05 |access-date=2012-08-05 |last=Roth |first=Matthew}} The bout was rescheduled for [[UFC on Fox 5]] on December 8, 2012.\n\n[[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] newcomer [[Roger Hollett]] was expected to face [[Matt Hamill]], who returned from retirement, at the event. However, Hollett was forced out of the bout due to a contract dispute and replaced by [[Vladimir Matyushenko]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30283/matyushenko-replaces-hollett-meets-hamill-at-ufc-152-in-toronto.mma |title=Matyushenko replaces Hollett, meets Hamill at UFC 152 in Toronto |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-08-20 |access-date=2012-08-20 |last=Stupp |first=Dann |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20130103214214/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/08/matyushenko-replaces-hollett-meets-hamill-at-ufc-152-in-toronto |archive-date=2013-01-03 }} Then, on September 11, 2012, Matyushenko pulled out of the fight after suffering a torn Achilles tendon while training, and Hamill vs. Hollett was rebooked by [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] officials.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30587/vladimir-matyushenko-injured-roger-hollett-back-in-to-meet-matt-hamill-at-ufc-152.mma |title=Vladimir Matyushenko injured; Roger Hollett back in to meet Matt Hamill at UFC 152 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-09-11 |access-date=2012-09-11 |last=Erickson |first=Matt |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20130129105320/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/09/vladimir-matyushenko-injured-roger-hollett-back-in-to-meet-matt-hamill-at-ufc-152 |archive-date=2013-01-29 }}\n\nA bout between [[Evan Dunham]] and [[T. J. Grant]] was originally supposed to be contested on the main card but was instead fought on the [[FX (TV channel)|FX]] prelims, for unknown reasons.\n\nAs a result of the cancellation of [[UFC 151]], a bout between [[Kyle Noke]] and [[Charlie Brenneman]] was rescheduled for this event.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30347/noke-brenneman-moved-to-ufc-152-in-toronto-miller-forced-out-of-bout-with-pierson.mma |title=Noke-Brenneman moved to UFC 152 in Toronto |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-08-23 |access-date=2012-08-23 |author=Staff |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20130103203226/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/08/noke-brenneman-moved-to-ufc-152-in-toronto-miller-forced-out-of-bout-with-pierson |archive-date=2013-01-03 }}\n\n[[Dan Miller (fighter)|Dan Miller]] was expected to face [[Sean Pierson]] at the event. However, Miller pulled out of the bout after getting news about his son's kidney transplant operation.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/dan-miller-happily-steps-off-ufc-152-fight-card-for-his-son |title=Dan Miller Happily Steps Off UFC 152 Fight Card for His Son |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |last=Pishna |first=Ken |date=2012-08-24 |access-date=2012-08-24}} He was replaced by [[Lance Benoist]].{{cite web |last=Erickson |first=Matt |date=2012-08-24 |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30368/with-miller-out-sean-pierson-now-meets-lance-benoist-at-ufc-152-in-toronto.mma |title=With Miller out, Sean Pierson now meets Lance Benoist at UFC 152 in Toronto |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |access-date=2012-08-25 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20130103193501/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/08/with-miller-out-sean-pierson-now-meets-lance-benoist-at-ufc-152-in-toronto |archive-date=2013-01-03 }}\n\nAt the weigh-ins, [[Charles Oliveira]] failed to make weight by four tenths of pound on his first try.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30710/ufc-152-live-and-official-weigh-in-results.mma |title=UFC 152 official weigh-in results: Dual title fights official, Oliveira just misses mark |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-09-21 |access-date=2012-09-21 |last=Staff |archive-date=2012-09-23 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120923004627/http://mmajunkie.com/news/30710/ufc-152-live-and-official-weigh-in-results.mma |url-status=dead }}\nIn the second, he was able to make 146.2 lb. However, his opponent signed to fight at a catchweight, and Oliveira was not fined.{{cite web |url=https://sports.yahoo.com/blogs/mma-cagewriter/ufc-152-fights-official-weigh-ins-210856236--mma.html |title=UFC 152 fights are official after weigh-ins |publisher=Yahoo! Sports |date=2012-09-21 |access-date=2012-09-21 |last=Hendricks |first=Maggie}}\n\n===Main event===\n{{see also|UFC 151}}\nThe main event was [[UFC Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Jon Jones]] facing former champion [[Vitor Belfort]]. Originally, Jones was set to defend his title against [[Dan Henderson]] at [[UFC 151]] on September 1, 2012, in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]], but [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] President [[Dana White]] announced on August 23, 2012, that Henderson had a tear in his [[Medial collateral ligament|MCL]] and would not be able to compete. It was then revealed that Jones declined a fight against [[Chael Sonnen]], who offered to take the fight on eight days notice. As a result, the event was cancelled. This marked the first time the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] had cancelled an event, with the official fight card having been announced, in the organization's history.\n{{Quote box| quote = \"Here I am with 35 years old, 17 years into my career, fighting the best fighter of all-time, in the history of the UFC, a guy who's [considered] the best athlete in MMA.\" |align=left |width=40% |source=-[[Vitor Belfort]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30418/ufc-152-title-challenger-vitor-belfort-im-fighting-the-best-fighter-of-all-time.mma |title=UFC 152 title challenger Vitor Belfort: 'I'm fighting the best fighter of all time' |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-08-29 |access-date=2012-08-29 |author=Staff |archive-date=2012-08-31 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120831073248/http://mmajunkie.com/news/30418/ufc-152-title-challenger-vitor-belfort-im-fighting-the-best-fighter-of-all-time.mma |url-status=dead }}}}\nFollowing the cancellation of the event, White said that [[UFC 151]] simply will not exist. Jones was set to face [[Lyoto Machida]] in a rematch at this event after the cancellation of that event, but Machida declined the fight. Subsequently, Belfort was chosen to take Machida's place.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30353/vitor-belfort-replaces-lyoto-machida-against-champ-jon-jones-at-ufc-152.mma |title=Vitor Belfort replaces Lyoto Machida against champ Jon Jones at UFC 152 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-08-24 |access-date=2012-08-24 |last=Marrocco |first=Steven |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20130103205408/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/08/vitor-belfort-replaces-lyoto-machida-against-champ-jon-jones-at-ufc-152 |archive-date=2013-01-03 }} Jones was a massive favorite coming into the bout.{{cite web |author=Stupp, Dann |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30361/the-oddessa-line-champ-jones-opens-as-13-to-1-favorite-over-belfort-at-ufc-152.mma |title=The Oddessa Line: Champ Jones opens as 13-to-1 favorite over Belfort at UFC 152 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-08-24 |access-date=2012-08-24 |url-status=dead It was later reported that Belfort fought Jones with a broken hand.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmamania.com/2012/9/23/3375776/ufc-152-results-vitor-belfort-reportedly-fought-jon-jones-with-a-broken-hand-mma |title=UFC 152 results: Vitor Belfort reportedly fought Jon Jones with a broken hand |publisher=MMAmania.com |date=2012-09-23 |access-date=2012-09-30 |last=Mrosko |first=Geno}} He also had elevated free testosterone levels found on a pre-fight test {{Cite St-Pierre]] was originally scheduled to defend his title in the Welterweight division against [[Nick Diaz]] in the main event. However, on December 7, 2011, it was revealed that St-Pierre was injured and could not fight. Diaz instead faced [[Carlos Condit]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/26422/champ-st-pierre-injured-diaz-vs-condit-for-interim-title-at-ufc-143.mma|title=Champ St-Pierre injured, Diaz vs.
