The card consisted of eleven bouts, with six televised live on [[pay-per-view]], four preliminary bouts on [[FX (TV channel)|FX]], and one on [[Facebook]].\n\nThe card's main event featured [[UFC Middleweight Championship|UFC Middleweight Champion]] [[Anderson Silva]] defending his title against [[Chael Sonnen]] in a highly anticipated bout.{{cite web|url=https://bleacherreport.com/articles/1223253-ufc-148-silva-vs-sonnen-ii-and-the-10-most-awaited-bouts-in-mma#/articles/1223253-ufc-148-silva-vs-sonnen-ii-and-the-10-most-awaited-bouts-in-mma|title=UFC 148 Silva vs. Sonnen II and the 10 most awaited bouts in MMA |publisher=Bleacher Report|date=2012-06-15|access-date=2012-10-01|last=Nile|first=Levi}}{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/is-silva-vs-sonnen-ii-the-most-anticipated-fight-in-ufc-history|title=Is Silva vs. Sonnen II the Most Anticipated Fight in UFC History?|publisher=MMAWeekly.com|date=2012-06-05|access-date=2012-10-01|last=Martin|first=Damon}} The contest was heavily promoted, featuring the International Fight Week, as well as the preview show ''[[UFC Primetime]]''.\n[[Tito Ortiz]] was inducted into the [[UFC Hall of Fame]] during the UFC Fan Expo in conjunction with the event,{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/29570/tito-ortiz-inducted-into-ufc-hall-of-fame-on-afternoon-of-final-fight-at-ufc-148.mma|title=Tito Ortiz inducted into UFC Hall of Fame on afternoon of final fight at UFC 148|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-07-07|access-date=2012-07-07|last1=Erickson|last2=Morgan|first1=Matt|first2=John|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120710115635/http://mmajunkie.com/news/29570/tito-ortiz-inducted-into-ufc-hall-of-fame-on-afternoon-of-final-fight-at-ufc-148.mma|archive-date=2012-07-10}} and fought fellow former [[UFC Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Forrest Griffin]] in the co-main event of the evening.\n\n==Background==\n\n===Card changes===\n[[Urijah Faber]] was expected to challenge [[Dominick Cruz]] for the [[UFC Bantamweight Championship]] in the co-main event (after their stint as opposing coaches on ''[[The Ultimate Fighter: Live]]''), but a torn [[anterior cruciate ligament|ACL]] forced Cruz off the card.{{cite web|url=http://www.lasvegassun.com/news/2012/may/07/dominick-cruz-out-ufc-148-with-torn-acl/|title=Dominick Cruz out of UFC 148 with torn ACL|publisher=Las Vegas Sun|date=2012-05-07|access-date=2012-10-02|last=Keefer|first=Case}} Faber was then expected to face replacement [[Renan Barão]] for the [[List of UFC champions#Bantamweight Championship|Interim UFC Bantamweight Championship]],{{cite web |url=https://sports.yahoo.com/news/urijah-fabers-opponent-ufc-146-031436363--mma.html|title=Urijah Faber's New Opponent at UFC 148 is ...|publisher=Yahoo! Sports|date=2012-05-18|access-date=2012-10-02|last=Martin|first=Damon|work=MMAWeekly.com}} but that fight was moved to the main event of ''[[2012 in UFC#UFC 149: Faber vs. Barão|UFC 149]]'' in [[Calgary, Alberta]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmamania.com/2012/6/9/3075584/jose-aldo-injured-urijah-faber-vs-renan-barao-fight-ufc-149-main-event |title=Jose Aldo injured, Urijah Faber vs Renan Barao fight moved to UFC 149 main event|publisher=MMAmania.com |date=2012-06-09|access-date=2012-10-02|last=Mrosko|first=Geno}} Barão was originally scheduled to fight [[Ivan Menjivar]] on the ''UFC 148'' preliminary card, and [[Mike Easton]] replaced Barão in the opening bout of the main card.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/ufc/2012/5/19/3031421/mike-easton-renan-barao-ivan-menjivar-ufc-148-mma-news|title=Mike Easton Replaces Renan Barao, Faces Ivan Menjivar at UFC 148|publisher=MMAFighting.com|date=2012-05-19|access-date=2012-10-02|last=Thomas|first=Luke}}\n\n[[Rich Franklin]] was expected to return to the [[Middleweight (MMA)|middleweight]] ranks in a bout against [[Cung Le]]. However, due to an injury sustained by ''[[UFC 147]]'' headliner [[Vitor Belfort]], Franklin was pulled from the bout with Le to replace Belfort in a rematch against [[Wanderlei Silva]].{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/blog/mma/post/_/id/11668/company-man-franklin-answers-call-again|title=Company man Franklin answers call again|publisher=ESPN|date=2012-06-01|last=Mindenhall|first=Chuck|access-date=2012-06-01}}\n\n===International Fight Week===\nIn conjunction with the [[Las Vegas Convention and Visitors Authority]], the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] planned five days of festivities (known as International Fight Week) leading up to ''UFC 148''. International Fight Week is expected to be an annual July occurrence, coinciding with a [[Las Vegas]] [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] event. [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] President [[Dana White]] stated, \"The first annual International Fight Week will be awesome, the fans are going to have a fight week experience better than anything they've ever had before, and each year we are going to build this thing to the point where every fan on the planet knows they have to come to Vegas for this July fight week at least once in their lives.\"{{cite web|url=http://www.lvrj.com/sports/international-fight-week-adds-to-ufc-148-hoopla-159537275.html|title=International Fight Week adds to UFC 148 hoopla|publisher=Las Vegas Review-Journal|date=2012-06-19|access-date=2012-06-19|last=Hill|first=Adam}} The UFC Fan Expo (two days of fighter [[Meet and greet|meet-and-greets]], training seminars, a [[Grapplers Quest]] tournament, [[Autograph|autograph sessions]] and other events) also occurred on July 6 and 7.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2012/1/26/2736412/ufc-fan-expo-2012-mandalay-bay-convention-center-las-vegas-nevada|title=Report: UFC Fan Expo 2012 slated for July 6–7 in Las Vegas|publisher=MMAmania.com|date=2012-01-26|access-date=2012-06-19|last=Holland|first=Jesse}}\n\n===Weigh-in===\n{{quote box|quote =I've got to give him [Sonnen] a lot of credit. He basically said to me that he wasn't looking for any kind of serious discipline against Anderson, that he'd forgiven him. He wasn't even sure if it was premeditated by Anderson. He was very gracious, very forgiving.|align=right|width=40%|source=-Keith Kizer, NSAC executive director.}}\n\nRegarded as the most notable weigh-in event in the history of [[combat sports]],{{cite video|title=UFC 148: Silva vs. Sonnen|medium=DVD|publisher=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]|location=[[Las Vegas, Nevada]], United States|date=2012-07-07}}\nit was held at the [[Mandalay Bay Events Center]] in [[Las Vegas]]. All fighters on the card made their required weight.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/29524/ufc-148-live-and-official-weigh-in-results.mma|title=UFC 148 official weigh-in results: Silva (184) and Sonnen (185) on weight|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-07-06|access-date=2012-10-01|author=Staff|archive-date=2012-07-10|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120710222354/http://mmajunkie.com/news/29524/ufc-148-live-and-official-weigh-in-results.mma|url-status=dead}} Silva threw a shoulder at [[Chael Sonnen]]. The move (which connected with Sonnen's face) led to a tense moment, when the two had to be separated by [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] president [[Dana White]].{{cite web|url=https://sports.yahoo.com/blogs/mma-cagewriter/anderson-silva-throws-shoulder-during-ufc-148-weigh-021207137--mma.html|title=Anderson Silva throws shoulder during UFC 148 weigh-in (VIDEO)|publisher=Yahoo! Sports|date=2012-07-06|access-date=2012-10-01|last=Hendricks|first=Maggie}} It was later reported by the [[Nevada State Athletic Commission]] that Silva would not be sanctioned for his actions.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/29601/anderson-silva-apologized-post-ufc-148-for-weigh-ins-incident-nsac-unlikely-to-punish.mma|title=Anderson Silva apologized post-UFC 148 for weigh-ins incident, NSAC unlikely to punish|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-07-09|access-date=2012-10-01|last=Marrocco|first=Steven|archive-date=2012-11-01|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121101192544/http://mmajunkie.com/news/29601/anderson-silva-apologized-post-ufc-148-for-weigh-ins-incident-nsac-unlikely-to-punish.mma|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Event==\nThe event consisted of 11 [[Mixed martial arts|mixed-martial-arts]] bouts sanctioned by the [[Nevada State Athletic Commission]] and contested under the [[Mixed martial arts rules#Unified Rules of Mixed Martial Arts|Unified Rules of Mixed Martial Arts]]. All bouts consisted of three five-minute rounds, except for the main-event championship match between Silva and Sonnen (which was scheduled for five five-minute rounds). Six bouts were televised live on [[pay-per-view]]. Four preliminary bouts were televised live on [[FX (TV channel)|FX]], and one was streamed live on [[Facebook]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/event/UFC148#/fight|title=UFC® 148 Live on Pay-Per-View Silva vs. Sonnen II|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|access-date=2012-06-27}} ''UFC 148'' set a [[Mixed martial arts|mixed-martial-arts]] gate record in [[Nevada]]. The event earned $6,901,655 in ticket sales, beating the previous record set at ''[[UFC 66]]''.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/29954/ufc-146-officially-draws-15104-attendance-for-6-9-million-live-gate.mma|title=UFC 148 officially draws 15,104 attendance for $6.9 million live gate|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-07-31|access-date=2012-07-31|author=Staff|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120802053013/http://mmajunkie.com/news/29954/ufc-146-officially-draws-15104-attendance-for-6-9-million-live-gate.mma|archive-date=2012-08-02}} Attendance was 15,104. It sold an estimated 925,000 [[pay-per-view]] buys, the most in 2012 for any [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] event.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2012/11/29/3702500/george-st-pierres-return-estimated-at-doing-680-700000-buys-for-ufc|title=George St-Pierre's return estimated at 680–700,000 buys for UFC 154 pay-per-view|publisher=MMAFighting.com|date=2012-11-29|access-date=2012-11-29|last=Meltzer|first=Dave}}\n\n===Preliminary card===\n[[Image:UFC-Octagon-USMCPhoto.jpg|left|thumb|All fights took place inside a cage, the UFC's ''Octagon''.]]\nThe event's five preliminary fights all went the full three rounds, ending in [[Mixed martial arts#Victory|decisions]]. In the first bout of the evening, [[Rafaello Oliveira]] outpointed Yoislandy Izquierdo to win 29–28 in all three judges' scorecards.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/UFC-148-Results-Live-PlaybyPlay-Updates-44001|title=UFC 148 RESULTS: LIVE PLAY-BY-PLAY & UPDATES|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=2012-07-07|access-date=2012-12-14}} After taking down Izquierdo early in the first round, Oliveira was hit by a strong knee in the following stanza, but put his opponent in the ground again. The final round saw both fighters [[grappling]], with Oliveira reigning victorious.\n\nIn the second bout, [[Shane Roller]] rebounded from three losses in a row with a unanimous decision victory over [[John Alessio]]. Roller, a former four-time [[Oklahoma]] high school state wrestling champion and a [[Big 12 Conference]] Champion at [[Oklahoma State University]],{{cite web|url=http://www.lasvegassun.com/news/2009/jul/31/round-two-roller/|title=Round two for Roller|publisher=Las Vegas Sun|date=2009-07-31|access-date=2012-12-14|last=Okamoto|first=Brett}} was able to take Alessio down and control his opponent's back on the ground, but was hurt on the feet in the first and third rounds. The three judges scored the fight 29–28 in favor of Roller.\n\nThe other [[Middleweight (MMA)|middleweight]] bout of the night saw [[Costas Philippou]] defeating [[Riki Fukuda]] by unanimous decision.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/29528/ufc-148-play-by-play-and-live-results|title=UFC 148 PLAY BY PLAY AND LIVE RESULTS|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-07-07|access-date=2012-12-14|author=Staff}}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }} Philippou successfully defended all of Fukuda's takedown attempts and landed the more solid strikes. The judges saw it 30–27, 30–27 and 29–28 for Philippou.\n\n[[Khabib Nurmagomedov]] defeated [[Gleison Tibau]] by unanimous decision in their [[Lightweight (MMA)|lightweight]] contest. In an uneventful bout, which saw only one successful takedown scored by Tibau despite a combined 19 attempts,{{cite web|url=http://ufcstats.com/fight-details/ded18af0e4eae2f7|title=UFC 148: Silva vs Sonnen 2}} Nurmagomedov won 30–27, 30–27 and 30–27 on all three of the judges scorecards. The most memorable part of this bout was the controversy surrounding the judges' decision.{{cite web|url=https://bleacherreport.com/articles/1250644-ufc-148-what-we-learned-from-gleison-tibau-vs-khabib-nurmagomedov|title=UFC 148: What We Learned from Gleison Tibau vs. Khabib Nurmagomedov|website=[[Bleacher Report]] }}\n\nIn the preliminary card's final match, [[Melvin Guillard]] faced [[Brazilian Jiu-Jitsu]] [[Black belt (martial arts)|black belt]] [[Fabrício Camões]]. Despite being mounted in the first round by Camoes, Guillard picked apart his opponent in the rest of the fight by exercising good takedown defense and outstriking Camoes. The judges awarded the [[Imperial Athletics]] representative the unanimous decision (30–27, 30–27, 30–27).\n\n===Main card===\nThe first bout on the [[pay-per-view]] portion of the event saw [[Mike Easton]] taking on [[Ivan Menjivar]] in a [[Bantamweight (MMA)|bantamweight]] contest. The entire fight remained on the feet, with both fighters throwing 317 combined strikes.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/By-the-Numbers-UFC-148-44017|title=BY THE NUMBERS: UFC 148|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=2012-07-08|access-date=2012-12-14|last=Critchfield|first=Tristen}} All three judges scored the bout in favor of Easton, with two scoring it 30–27 while one judge seeing it 29–28 giving Easton the unanimous decision victory. \n\nThe second bout saw former title contender [[Chad Mendes]] fighting [[The Ultimate Fighter: Team GSP vs. Team Koscheck]] contestant [[Cody McKenzie]]. Early in the first round, Mendes caught his opponent with a straight right hand to the midsection. McKenzie collapsed to his knees and Mendes attacked him with [[ground-and-pound]], forcing the referee to stop the contest at thirty-one seconds. It was the first knockout victory for Mendes since 2009.\n\nIn his first fight as a [[Welterweight (MMA)|welterweight]], former [[UFC Middleweight Championship|UFC Middleweight title]] challenger [[Demian Maia]] faced [[Judo]] black belt [[Dong Hyun Kim]]. The bout was stopped in forty-seven seconds by referee [[Mario Yamasaki]], after Maia took Kim down and finished him with strikes from the [[mounted position]]. [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] commentator [[Joe Rogan]] said that Kim had broken a rib, but it was later revealed that he suffered a major muscle spasm.{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2012/7/9/3147265/ufc-148-dong-hyun-kim-muscle-spasm-broken-rib-maia-mma-news|title=UFC 148: Dong Hyun Kim Suffered Muscle Spasm, Not Broken Rib|publisher=BllodyElbow.com|date=2012-07-09|access-date=2012-12-18|last=Burke|first=Tim|archive-date=2012-10-16|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121016092644/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2012/7/9/3147265/ufc-148-dong-hyun-kim-muscle-spasm-broken-rib-maia-mma-news|url-status=dead}}\n\nThe fourth fight featured the former [[List of Strikeforce champions#Middleweight Championship|Strikeforce Middleweight Champion]] [[Cung Le]] meeting veteran [[Patrick Côté (fighter)|Patrick Côté]] at [[Middleweight (MMA)|middleweight]].{{cite web|last=Holland|first=Jesse|date=2012-06-19|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2012/6/19/3097410/ufc-148-fight-card-six-fights-july-7-las-vegas|title=UFC 148 fight card complete with six main card fights for July 7 in Las Vegas |publisher=MMAmania.com|access-date=2012-12-22}} This was Le's second fight in the promotion, after losing his first to [[Wanderlei Silva]] by TKO at ''[[UFC 139]]''. Côté was returning to the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] after last losing a decision to [[Tom Lawlor]] at ''[[UFC 121]]'' in October 2010. He was on a four-fight winning streak, undefeated since leaving the organization.{{cite web|url=https://lfpress.com/sports/othersports/2012/06/02/19829961.html|title=Patrick Cote returns to UFC in a big way|publisher=London Free Press|date=2012-06-02|access-date=2012-06-02|last=Springer|first=Neil|archive-date=2014-05-13|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140513143710/http://www.lfpress.com/sports/othersports/2012/06/02/19829961.html|url-status=dead}} Le won a hard-fought unanimous decision by exercising great footwork, trips and takedowns and effective striking.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/28968/cung-le-vs-patrick-cote-targeted-for-ufc-148.mma|title=Cung Le vs. Patrick Cote targeted for UFC 148|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-05-30|access-date=2012-05-30|author=Staff|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120601161351/http://mmajunkie.com/news/28968/cung-le-vs-patrick-cote-targeted-for-ufc-148.mma|archive-date=2012-06-01}}\n\nHours before the long-awaited rubber match and co-main event of the evening between former [[UFC Light Heavyweight Champion]]s [[Tito Ortiz]] and [[Forrest Griffin]], Ortiz was inducted into the [[UFC Hall of Fame]]. The history of the two fighters started when Ortiz won their first bout at ''[[UFC 59]]'', and Griffin won the rematch at ''[[UFC 106]]''; both fights ended in a split decision.{{cite web|url=https://www.usatoday.com/sports/mma/post/2012/03/651532/1|title=Tito Ortiz: Griffin fight set for July 7|publisher=USA TODAY|date=2012-03-20|access-date=2012-10-02}}{{cite web|url=http://www.mmamania.com/2012/7/8/3144660/ufc-148-results-recap-forrest-griffin-vs-tito-ortiz-3-fight-review|title=UFC 148 results recap: Forrest Griffin vs Tito Ortiz 3 fight review and analysis|publisher=MMAmania.com|date=2012-07-08|access-date=2013-01-21|last=Hemminger|first=Brian}} As in the first two fights, Ortiz came out strong, but Griffin countered his attacks and ultimately won via unanimous decision.\n\n====Silva vs. Sonnen====\nThe sixth and final bout of the main card was the [[UFC Middleweight Championship]] match between [[Anderson Silva|Anderson SIlva]] and [[Chael Sonnen]], regulated by referee Yves Lavigne.{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/NAC-Appoints-Yves-Lavigne-to-Officiate-SonnenSilva-Rematch-at-UFC-148-43503|title=NAC APPOINTS YVES LAVIGNE TO OFFICIATE SONNEN-SILVA REMATCH AT UFC 148|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=2012-06-15|access-date=2013-01-17|last=Critchfield|first=Tristen}}{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/06/nsac-selects-referee-yves-lavigne-for-ufc-148s-silva-sonnen-ii-headliner|title=NSAC SELECTS REFEREE YVES LAVIGNE FOR UFC 148'S SILVA-SONNEN II HEADLINER|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-06-14|access-date=2013-01-17|last=Stupp|first=Dann}}{{Dead link|date=January 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }} \nThe fight was a rematch of the main event at ''[[UFC 117]]'', also a contest for the [[UFC Middleweight Championship]]. In that match, Silva (after losing four rounds on points) made Sonnen submit to a triangle choke with 1:50 left in the fight. Sonnen landed 320 strikes against Silva, more than any other fighter since ''[[UFC 5]]'' (when [[Royce Gracie]] landed 355 strikes against [[Ken Shamrock]]). Silva had only absorbed 166 strikes (combined) in his previous 11 matches.{{cite web|url=https://sports.yahoo.com/news/mma--chael-sonnen-raises-anderson-silva-s-ire-ahead-of-ufc-148--and-maybe-that-s-the-way-he-planned-it.html|title=Chael Sonnen raises Anderson Silva's ire ahead of UFC 148, and that's exactly the way he planned it|publisher=Yahoo! Sports|date=2012-06-28|access-date=2012-06-28|last=Iole|first=Kevin}} With the win Silva retained the [[UFC Middleweight Championship]], his undefeated record in the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]] and extended his winning streak to 13. The bout received the Fight of the Year award at the 2010 [[World MMA Awards]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2010/12/01/world-mma-awards-2010-winners|title=World MMA Awards 2010 Winners|publisher=MMAFighting.com|date=2010-12-01|access-date=2012-05-18|last=Tucker|first=Bryan}} After ''[[UFC 117]]'' Silva defended the title twice, with KO and TKO wins over [[Vitor Belfort]] and [[Yushin Okami]] respectively. Sonnen is also undefeated since ''[[UFC 117]]'', defeating [[Brian Stann]] and [[Michael Bisping]] and setting the stage for the most-anticipated rematch of the year.{{cite web|url=http://theadvocate.com/entertainment/mixedmartialarts/3277358-123/ufc-148-the-most-anticipated|title=UFC 148: The most anticipated fight of the year|publisher=The Advocate|date=2012-09-22|access-date=2012-10-02|last=Ramsay|first=Matt|archive-date=2012-10-16|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121016084130/http://theadvocate.com/entertainment/mixedmartialarts/3277358-123/ufc-148-the-most-anticipated|url-status=dead}}\n\nThe [[UFC Middleweight Championship]] main-event match between Silva and Sonnen was originally scheduled for ''[[UFC 147]]'' at [[Estádio Olímpico João Havelange|Havelange Stadium]] in [[Rio de Janeiro]], [[Brazil]]. Due to a scheduling conflict with the [[United Nations Conference on Sustainable Development]], the bout was moved to headline ''UFC 148'' at the [[MGM Grand Garden Arena]] in [[Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]].{{cite web|url=http://www.lasvegassun.com/news/2012/apr/24/anderson-silva-vs-chael-sonnen-ii-moved-las-vegas-/|title=Anderson Silva vs. Chael Sonnen II moved to Las Vegas for UFC 148|publisher=Las Vegas Sun|date=2012-04-24|access-date=2012-10-02|last=Keefer|first=Case}} At this point in their careers Sonnen was ranked as the number-two [[Middleweight (MMA)|middleweight]] in the world by [[Sherdog]],{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/rankings/4/Sherdogs-Official-Mixed-Martial-Arts-Rankings-42147|title=Sherdog Middleweight Rankings|publisher=Sherdog.com|date=2012-05-18|access-date=2012-05-18|author=Staff}} while Silva is ranked number-one middleweight and the number-one [[pound-for-pound]] fighter in the world according to [[Sherdog]], [[Yahoo! Sports]], and [[ESPN]].{{cite web|url=https://www.espn.com/mma/|title=MMA Power Rankings|publisher=ESPN|access-date=2012-05-18}}{{cite web|url=https://sports.yahoo.com/mma/news?slug=dd-mmaranks021711|title=Rankings: Silva vs. GSP| publisher=Yahoo! Sports|date=2011-02-17|access-date=2012-05-18|last=Doyle|first=Dave}}{{cite web|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/rankings/Sherdogcoms-Pound-for-Pound-Top-10-35177|title=Sherdog.com's Pound-for-Pound Top 10|date=2011-08-30|publisher=Sherdog.com|access-date=2012-05-18}} In an effort to further promote the contest, ''[[UFC Primetime]]'' returned on [[Fox Sports 2|Fuel TV]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/29452/reminder-ufc-148-primetime-debuts-tonight-on-fuel-tv.mma|title=Reminder: 'UFC 148 Primetime' debuts tonight on FUEL TV|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-06-30|access-date=2012-12-09|last=Morgan|first=John|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20130111051155/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/06/reminder-ufc-148-primetime-debuts-tonight-on-fuel-tv|archive-date=2013-01-11}}\n[[File:LasVegas-MGMgrand.jpg|thumb|left|alt=UFC 148 took place at the MGM Grand Garden Arena, in Las Vegas, Nevada, the UFC's hometown|UFC 148 took place at the MGM Grand Garden Arena.]]\n\nAfter Sonnen's win over [[Nate Marquardt]] in early 2010, Sonnen became a polarizing figure due to his criticism of Silva and overall [[trash talk]] (including comments directed towards Silva's manager, teammates, coaches, wife and home country of [[Brazil]]). Silva remained relatively quiet until a ''UFC 148'' media call in June 2012, when he stated through an interpreter: \"Chael Sonnen's going to get his ass kicked like he's never gotten his ass kicked before. What I'm going to do inside the Octagon is something that's going to change the image of the sport. This is going to be violent and I am sorry. I'm going to make sure that every one of his teeth are broken, that his arms are broken and his legs are broken. He's not going to be able to walk out of the Octagon by himself. I can guarantee that. He will need a plastic surgeon afterwards. And I know that he's listening, so the game's over. No more shit talking. It's on now.\" This caught some by surprise, who perceived his remarks as out of character.{{cite web|url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/sports/more-sports/death-threats-are-out-but-breaking-teeth-and-limbs-appears-ok/article4378129/|title=Death threats are out but breaking teeth and limbs appears OK|publisher=The Globe and Mail|date=2012-06-28|access-date=2012-06-28|last=Davidson|first=Neil}}\n\nAs the fight finally took place, Sonnen quickly took the champion down in the first round and maintained a dominant position throughout, eventually gaining full mount while attacking with [[ground-and-pound]].\nOne cageside judge scored it a 10–8 round as Silva was credited with zero strikes.{{cite web |url=http://sportsillustrated.cnn.com/2012/writers/jeff_wagenheim/07/08/ufc-148/index.html?eref=sircrc |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120711000236/http://sportsillustrated.cnn.com/2012/writers/jeff_wagenheim/07/08/ufc-148/index.html?eref=sircrc |url-status=dead |archive-date=July 11, 2012 |title=Once a dominant fighter, Anderson Silva is now truly a great one |publisher=Sports Illustrated |date=2012-07-08 |access-date=2012-07-08 |last=Wagenheim |first=Jeff}}\n Sonnen connected with 76 strikes from the top position in the opening round, and held a 22-to-15 edge in significant strikes against Silva. However, Sonnen was unable to inflict substantial damage to the champion, and as the fight resumed Silva was repeatedly admonished by the ref for holding Chael's shorts. Being unable to score a takedown due to Silva's takedown defense, Sonnen failed to connect with a high-risk spinning backfist strike, leaving Sonnen in a scooting position for Silva to land a knee to the chest, then with punches to stop Sonnen with a TKO at 1:55 of the second round.{{cite web|last=Iole|first=Kevin|title=Anderson Silva quiets Chael Sonnen, leaving only Jon Jones as a worthy challenger|url=https://ca.sports.yahoo.com/news/mma--anderson-silva-quiets-chael-sonnen--leaving-jon-jones-as-only-challenge-left.html|publisher=Yahoo! Sports|access-date=2012-07-08|date=2012-07-08}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Anderson Silva]] (c)\n|def.\n|[[Chael Sonnen]]\n|TKO (knee |title=UFC 150: Henderson vs. Edgar II |publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship |access-date=2012-06-06}}\n\n==Background==\n\nA fight between [[Dennis Hallman]] and [[Thiago Tavares]] was expected for this card. However, on July 12 it was announced that the fight was moved to the [[UFC 151]] card.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/29661/dennis-hallman-vs-thiago-tavares-moves-from-ufc-150-to-ufc-151.mma |title=Dennis Hallman vs. Thiago Tavares moves from UFC 150 to UFC 151 |publisher=[[MMAjunkie.com]] |date=2012-07-12 |access-date=2012-08-12 |author=Marrocco, Steven |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20130104045812/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/07/dennis-hallman-vs-thiago-tavares-moves-from-ufc-150-to-ufc-151 |archive-date=2013-01-04 }}\n\n[[Luiz Cane]] was expected to face [[Yushin Okami]] at this event. However, Cane was forced out of the bout with an injury and replaced by [[Rousimar Palhares]]. Palhares himself was forced out of the bout with Okami with an injury and was replaced by Buddy Roberts. Roberts was already on the card, originally scheduled against [[Chris Camozzi]], but Camozzi was also forced off the card with an injury.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/rousimar-palhares-out-buddy-roberts-in-against-yushin-okami-at-ufc-150 |title=Rousimar Palhares Out; Buddy Roberts In Against Yushin Okami at UFC 150 |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |last=Martin |first=Damon |access-date=2012-06-06}}\n\nAt the weigh-ins, [[Melvin Guillard]] was the only one of 20 fighters who missed weight. Guillard, who fought former training partner [[Donald Cerrone]], weighed 157.5 pounds on his first try. He was given two hours to cut to the [[Lightweight (MMA)|lightweight]] maximum of 156 pounds, but he elected instead to surrender an undisclosed percentage of his fight purse to Cerrone, who weighed 155 pounds.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30068/ufc-150-live-and-official-weigh-in-results.mma |title=UFC 150 weigh-in results: Henderson and Edgar on weight, Guillard comes in heavy However, Taylor was forced out of the bout with an injury and replaced by [[Rafael dos Anjos]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmamania.com/2012/5/22/3037786/anthony-njokuani-vs-rafael-dos-anjos-fight-ufc-fuel-tv-4-paul-taylor-injured-mma |title=Anthony Njokuani vs Rafael dos Anjos fight planned for UFC on FUEL TV 4 with Paul Taylor hurt |publisher=MMAmania.com |access-date=May 22, 2012}}\n\n[[Jon Fitch]] was expected to face [[Aaron Simpson (fighter)|Aaron Simpson]] at the event. However, Fitch was forced out of the bout, citing a knee injury and replaced by [[Kenny Robertson]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/06/with-jon-fitch-out-aaron-simpson-draws-kenny-robertson-at-ufc-on-fuel-tv-4 |title=With Jon Fitch out, Aaron Simpson draws Kenny Robertson at UFC on FUEL TV 4 |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |access-date=June 5, 2012 |archive-date=March 22, 2013 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130322020948/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/06/with-jon-fitch-out-aaron-simpson-draws-kenny-robertson-at-ufc-on-fuel-tv-4 |url-status=dead }}\n\n[[Brandon Vera]] was expected to face [[James Te Huna]] at the event. }}\n\n==Background==\n[[Jörgen Kruth]] was expected to make his UFC debut against [[Fabio Maldonado]] at this event. However, Kruth dropped out of the fight to retire from mixed martial arts. As a result, Maldonado was pulled from the event and faced [[Glover Teixeira]] at [[UFC 153]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30610/usa-today-anderson-silva-vs-stephan-bonnar-headlines-ufc-153.mma/|title=USA TODAY: Anderson Silva vs. Stephan Bonnar headlines UFC 153|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|work=[[USA TODAY]]|date=2012-09-07|access-date=2012-09-12|author=Morgan, John|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120914053922/http://mmajunkie.com/news/30610/usa-today-anderson-silva-vs-stephan-bonnar-headlines-ufc-153.mma|archive-date=2012-09-14}}\n\n[[Pascal Krauss]] was expected to face promotional newcomer [[Gunnar Nelson (fighter)|Gunnar Nelson]] at the event. However, Krauss was forced out of the bout with an injury and Nelson was briefly linked to fight with [[Rich Attonito]] in a [[catchweight]] bout to be contested at 175 lb.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30658/replacement-rich-attonito-meets-gunnar-nelson-at-ufc-on-fuel-tv-5.mma|title=Replacement Rich Attonito meets Gunnar Nelson at UFC on FUEL TV 5|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-09-17|access-date=2012-09-17|author=Staff|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120919215809/http://mmajunkie.com/news/30658/replacement-rich-attonito-meets-gunnar-nelson-at-ufc-on-fuel-tv-5.mma|archive-date=2012-09-19}} However, Attonito had reservations about taking the short notice bout due to the weight cut requirements. [[DaMarques Johnson]] was slated to take on Nelson at catch weight 175 lb,{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/debuting-gunnar-nelson-now-draws-damarques-johnson-at-ufc-on-fuel-tv-5|title=Debuting Gunnar Nelson Now Draws DaMarques Johnson at UFC on Fuel TV 5 |publisher=MMAWeekly.com |date=2012-09-18|access-date=2012-09-18|author=Staff}}\nbut DaMarques missed the weight by 8 lb, while Gunnar weighted at 175 lb. fight between [[John Dodson (fighter)|John Dodson]] and [[Jussier Formiga]] was a title elimination bout with the winner being the first man to challenge the newly crowned [[UFC Flyweight Championship|UFC Flyweight champion]], [[Demetrious Johnson (fighter)|Demetrious Johnson]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30915/title-shot-officially-goes-to-ufc-on-fx-5s-john-dodson-jussier-formiga-winner.mma|title=Title shot officially goes to UFC on FX 5's John Dodson-Jussier Formiga bout between [[Dennis Hallman]] and [[Thiago Tavares]] was expected to take place at this event; however, UFC officials cancelled the fight before the fighters stepped on the scale, because Hallman had missed weight significantly. Bigfoot]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC on Fuel TV: Franklin vs. Le]]\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]}}\n'''''UFC 153: Silva vs. Bonnar''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] [[pay-per-view]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on October 13, 2012, at the [[HSBC Arena (Rio de Janeiro)|HSBC Arena]] in [[Rio de Janeiro]], [[Brazil]].\n\n==Background==\n[[Vitor Belfort]] was expected to face [[Alan Belcher]] at the event. However, Belfort was pulled from the bout to face Light Heavyweight Champion [[Jon Jones]] on September 22, 2012 at [[UFC 152]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30353/vitor-belfort-replaces-lyoto-machida-against-champ-jon-jones-at-ufc-152.mma|title=Vitor Belfort replaces Lyoto Machida against champ Jon Jones at UFC 152|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-08-24|access-date=2012-08-24|author=Marrocco, Steven|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20130103205408/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/08/vitor-belfort-replaces-lyoto-machida-against-champ-jon-jones-at-ufc-152|archive-date=2013-01-03}} Belcher also was forced to drop out of the card due to a spinal fracture.{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2012/8/24/3266354/mma-news-ufc-mw-alan-belcher-out-ufc-153-injury-wants-chris-weidman|title=Alan Belcher Out Of UFC 153 Due To Spinal Fracture|publisher=BloodyElbow.com|date=2012-08-24|access-date=2012-08-25|author=Alexander, Mookie|archive-date=2012-08-25|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120825044132/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2012/8/24/3266354/mma-news-ufc-mw-alan-belcher-out-ufc-153-injury-wants-chris-weidman|url-status=dead}}\n\nAfter having their first fight end in a no contest due to an eye poke in the first round, a rematch between [[Phil Davis (fighter)|Phil Davis]] and [[Wagner Prado]] was briefly linked to [[UFC on FX: Browne vs. Bigfoot]] and then scheduled for this event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30373/with-belchers-spinal-fracture-davis-vs-prado-shifts-to-ufc-153-in-rio.mma|title=With Belcher's spinal fracture, Davis vs. Prado shifts to UFC 153 in Rio|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-08-25|access-date=2012-08-24|author=Staff|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20130103203400/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/08/with-belchers-spinal-fracture-davis-vs-prado-shifts-to-ufc-153-in-rio|archive-date=2013-01-03}}\n\n[[Rampage Jackson|Quinton Jackson]] was scheduled to face [[Glover Teixeira]] at the event. However, Jackson pulled out of the bout citing an injury,{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30586/quinton-rampage-jackson-hurt-forced-out-of-ufc-153-co-main-event-vs-glover-teixeira.mma|title=Quinton 'Rampage' Jackson hurt, forced out of UFC 153 co-main event vs. Glover Teixeira|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-09-11|access-date=2012-09-11|author=Erickson, Matt|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20130129001740/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/09/quinton-rampage-jackson-hurt-forced-out-of-ufc-153-co-main-event-vs-glover-teixeira|archive-date=2013-01-29}} and was replaced by [[Fabio Maldonado]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmamania.com/2012/9/12/3324436/ufc-153-fabio-maldonado-replaces-quinton-jackson-fights-glover-teixeira|title=Fabio Maldonado Fills In For Quinton Jackson, Set To Fight Glover Teixeira In Co-Main Event Of UFC 153 |publisher=MMAmania.com |date=2012-09-12 |access-date=2012-09-12 |last=Halvatzis Jr. |first=George}} Former [[UFC Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Rashad Evans]] was offered the fight with Teixeira but turned it down due to the short notice.{{cite web|title=Rashad explains Teixeira rejection|url=http://www.espn.co.uk/ufc/sport/story/172723.html|publisher=ESPN|date=2012-10-01|access-date=2012-10-01|author=Staff}}\n\nGeronimo dos Santos was scheduled to fight [[Gabriel Gonzaga]] at this event. However, dos Santos pulled out of the bout after a blood test showed that he had [[antibodies]] for [[Hepatitis B]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30968/heavyweight-geronimo-dos-santos-out-at-ufc-153-gabriel-gonzaga-without-opponent.mma|title=Heavyweight Geronimo dos Santos out at UFC 153, Gabriel Gonzaga without opponent|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-10-04|access-date=2012-10-04|author=Staff|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20130129003441/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/10/heavyweight-geronimo-dos-santos-out-at-ufc-153-gabriel-gonzaga-without-opponent|archive-date=2013-01-29}}{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/31010/saturdays-ufc-153-lineup-set-with-two-facebook-prelims-gonzaga-off-card.mma|title=Saturday's UFC 153 lineup set with two Facebook prelims, Gonzaga off card|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-10-07|access-date=2012-10-07|author=Staff|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20130128211511/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/10/saturdays-ufc-153-lineup-set-with-two-facebook-prelims-gonzaga-off-card|archive-date=2013-01-28}}{{cite web|title=Fora do UFC Rio III, Mondragon terá de fazer tratamento por um ano|url=http://sportv.globo.com/site/eventos/combate/noticia/2012/10/fora-do-ufc-rio-iii-mondragon-tera-de-fazer-tratamento-por-um-ano.html|publisher=SporTV.com|date=2012-10-09|access-date=2012-10-10}} With no time to find a suitable replacement, Gonzaga was pulled from the card.\n\n[[Erik Koch]] was expected to face [[List of UFC champions#Featherweight Championship|UFC Featherweight Champion]] [[José Aldo]] in a title fight at this event, but on August 31, 2012, Koch was forced to pull out due to injury. Former [[UFC Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight Champion]] [[Frankie Edgar]] had agreed to step in for Koch and fight Aldo for the Featherweight title.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30447/usa-today-frankie-edgar-replaces-erik-koch-meets-champ-jose-aldo-at-ufc-153.mma|title=USA TODAY: Frankie Edgar replaces Erik Koch, meets champ Jose Aldo at UFC 153|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|work=USA TODAY|date=2012-08-30|access-date=2012-08-31|last=Morgan|first=John|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20121216095759/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/30447/usa-today-frankie-edgar-replaces-erik-koch-meets-champ-jose-aldo-at-ufc-153|archive-date=2012-12-16}} However, on September 11, Aldo was also forced to pull out of the event with a foot injury after being involved in a motorcycle accident.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30588/injury-knocks-jose-aldo-out-of-ufc-153-featherweight-title-main-event-with-frankie-edgar.mma|title=Injury knocks Jose Aldo out of UFC 153 featherweight title main event with Frankie Edgar|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-09-11|access-date=2012-09-11|last=Erickson|first=Matt|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://archive.today/20121208224303/http://mmajunkie.com/news/30588/injury-knocks-jose-aldo-out-of-ufc-153-featherweight-title-main-event-with-frankie-edgar|archive-date=2012-12-08}}\n\nOn September 12, 2012, UFC officials announced that [[UFC Middleweight Championship|UFC Middleweight Champion]] [[Anderson Silva]] and former [[The Ultimate Fighter]] star [[Stephan Bonnar]] would fight in the main event.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30610/usa-today-anderson-silva-vs-stephan-bonnar-headlines-ufc-153.mma |title=USA TODAY: Anderson Silva vs. Stephan Bonnar headlines UFC 153 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-09-12 |access-date=2012-09-13 |last=Morgan |first=John |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20130129032611/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/09/usa-today-anderson-silva-vs-stephan-bonnar-headlines-ufc-153 |archive-date=2013-01-29 }} Silva was a massive favorite going into the bout,{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30620/anderson-silva-opens-as-more-than-13-to-1-favorite-vs-stephan-bonnar-at-ufc-153.mma |title=Anderson Silva opens as more |archive-url=https://archive.today/20130128222048/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/09/anderson-silva-opens-as-more-than-13-to-1-favorite-vs-stephan-bonnar-at-ufc-153 |archive-date=2013-01-28 }} which was a three-round fight. Although all main event fights have been five-round affairs since [[UFC 138]], UFC 153 was an exception due to the short notice, along with [[UFC 234]], when [[Robert Whittaker (fighter)|Robert Whittaker]] pulled out of the event a few hours prior.{{Cite web |date=2019-02-09 |title=Whittaker off UFC card after emergency surgery |url=https://www.espn.com/mma/ufc/story/_/id/25959907/middleweight-champion-robert-whittaker-ruled-title-defense-fight |access-date=2025-01-10 |website=ESPN.com |language=en}}{{cite web |url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2012/9/13/3324786/ufc-153-anderson-silva-vs-stephan-bonnar-3-rounds-mma-news |title=UFC 153: Anderson Silva Vs. Stephan Bonnar Will Be A 3 Round Fight |publisher=BloodyElbow.com |date=2012-09-13 |access-date=2012-09-13 |last=Hall |first=Chris |archive-date=2012-09-14 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120914201130/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2012/9/13/3324786/ufc-153-anderson-silva-vs-stephan-bonnar-3-rounds-mma-news |url-status=dead }}\n\nOn November 4, 2012, UFC announced Bonnar had tested positive for [[drostanolone]], and [[Dave_Herman_(fighter)|Dave Herman]] for [[marijuana]]. Both fighters were suspended.[http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Stephan-Bonnar-Dave-Herman-Fail-UFC-153-Drug-Tests-47019 \"Stephan Bonnar, Dave Herman Fail UFC 153 Drug Tests\", from [[Takeya Mizugaki]] vs. [[Jeff Hougland]] and [[John Lineker]] vs. [[Yasuhiro Urushitani]] were rescheduled for this event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30369/mizugaki-hougland-urushitani-lineker-move-from-ufc-151-to-ufc-on-fuel-tv-6-in-macau.mma|title=Mizugaki-Hougland, Urushitani-Lineker move from UFC 151 to UFC on Guimarães]] was expected to face [[Hyun Gyu Lim]] at the event; however, Guimarães was forced out of the bout with an injury and replaced by [[David Mitchell (fighter)|David Mitchell]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/31284/david-mitchell-vs-hyun-gyu-lim-completes-ufc-on-fuel-tv-6-lineup-in-macau.mma|title=David Mitchell vs. Hyun Gyu Lim completes UFC on FUEL TV 6 lineup in Lim was then pulled from the bout by doctors and the fight was subsequently cancelled.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/31518/david-mitchell-awarded-show-money-after-hyun-gyu-lim-pulled-from-ufc-on-fuel-tv-6.mma|title=David Mitchell awarded show money after Hyun Gyu Lim pulled from UFC Championship]]}}\n\n'''''UFC on Fox: Henderson vs. Diaz''''' (also known as '''''UFC on Fox 5''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event that was held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] on December 8, 2012, at [[KeyArena]] in [[Seattle, Washington]]. It was broadcast live on [[Fox Sports (United States)|Fox Sports]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30312/ufc-on-fox-5-henderson-vs-diaz-to-take-place-at-seattles-keyarena.mma |title='UFC on FOX 5: Henderson vs. Diaz' to take place Dec. 8 at Seattle's KeyArena |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-08-21 |access-date=2012-08-21 |author=Staff |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120825004117/http://mmajunkie.com/news/30312/ufc-on-fox-5-henderson-vs-diaz-to-take-place-at-seattles-keyarena.mma |archive-date=2012-08-25 }}\n\n==Background==\nAs a result of the cancellation of [[UFC 151]], bouts between [[Tim Means]] vs. [[Abel Trujillo]], [[Daron Cruickshank]] vs. [[Henry Martinez (fighter)|Henry Martinez]]{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/30399/means-trujillo-cruickshank-martinez-moved-from-scrapped-ufc-151-to-ufc-on-fox-5.mma|title=Means-Trujillo, Cruickshank-Martinez moved from scrapped UFC 151 to UFC on FOX 5|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-08-27|access-date=2012-08-27|author=Staff|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120830070032/http://mmajunkie.com/news/30399/means-trujillo-cruickshank-martinez-moved-from-scrapped-ufc-151-to-ufc-on-fox-5.mma|archive-date=2012-08-30}}\nand [[Dennis Siver]] vs. Eddie Yagin were rescheduled for this event.{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2012/8/30/3279787/mma-news-ufc-151-rescheduled-eddie-yagin-vs-dennis-siver-moved-ufc-on-fox-5|title=Dennis Siver Vs. Eddie Yagin Moved To UFC On FOX 5, All UFC 151 Fights Now Rescheduled|publisher=BloodyElbow.com|date=2012-08-30|access-date=2012-08-30|last=Alexander|first=Mookie|archive-date=2012-09-02|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120902231844/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2012/8/30/3279787/mma-news-ufc-151-rescheduled-eddie-yagin-vs-dennis-siver-moved-ufc-on-fox-5|url-status=dead}}\n\n[[Rafaello Oliveira]] was expected to face [[Michael Chiesa]] at the event. However, Oliveira was forced out of the bout with a broken hand, and replaced by [[Marcus LeVesseur]].{{cite web |author=Staff |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/31274/replacement-marcus-levesseur-meets-michael-chiesa-at-ufc-on-fox-5.mma |title=Replacement Marcus LeVesseur meets Michael Chiesa at UFC on FOX 5 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-10-24 |access-date=2012-10-24 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121026031605/http://mmajunkie.com/news/31274/replacement-marcus-levesseur-meets-michael-chiesa-at-ufc-on-fox-5.mma |archive-date=2012-10-26 }} Then, the week of the event, Chiesa was forced out of the bout with LeVesseur with an undisclosed illness and the bout was scrapped altogether.{{cite web |author=Staff |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/2012/12/michael-chiesa-ill-scratches-from-saturdays-ufc-on-fox-5-event |title=Michael Chiesa ill, scratches from Saturday's UFC on FOX 5 event |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-12-03 |access-date=2012-12-03 |archive-date=2012-12-05 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121205180706/http://mmajunkie.com/news/2012/12/michael-chiesa-ill-scratches-from-saturdays-ufc-on-fox-5-event |url-status=dead }} Also, on the day of the weigh-ins for the event, [[Tim Means]] was forced from his bout with [[Abel Trujillo]] after sustaining a head injury caused by a fall in a hotel sauna. LeVesseur replaced Means in the bout.{{cite web |last=Brand |first=Jeremy |url=http://www.mmasucka.com/news/tim-means-marcus-levesseur-ufc-fox-5-abel-trujillo/ |title=Tim Means out, Marcus LeVesseur in at UFC on FOX 5 against Abel Trujillo\n|publisher=MMAsucka.com |date=2012-12-07 |access-date=2012-12-07}}\n\n[[T.J. Dillashaw]] was scheduled to fight [[Mike Easton]] in the preliminary card. However, an injury sidelined Dillashaw, who was forced out of the bout and replaced by [[Bryan Caraway]].{{cite web |author=Staff |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/31355/bryan-caraway-in-for-injured-t-j-dillashaw-faces-mike-easton-at-ufc-on-fox-5.mma |title=Bryan Caraway in for injured T.J. Dillashaw, faces Mike Easton at UFC on FOX 5 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-10-30 |access-date=2012-10-30 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121101100024/http://mmajunkie.com/news/31355/bryan-caraway-in-for-injured-t-j-dillashaw-faces-mike-easton-at-ufc-on-fox-5.mma |archive-date=2012-11-01 }} Then, on November 21, it was announced that Caraway was forced out of the bout with an injury and replaced by [[Raphael Assunção]].{{cite web |author=Staff |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/31750/raphael-assuncao-replaces-bryan-caraway-faces-mike-easton-at-ufc-on-fox-5.mma |title=Raphael Assuncao replaces Bryan Caraway, faces Mike Easton at UFC on FOX 5 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-11-21 |access-date=2012-11-21 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://archive.today/20130103225344/http://mmajunkie.com/news/31750/raphael-assuncao-replaces-bryan-caraway-faces-mike-easton-at-ufc-on-fox-5.mma |archive-date=2013-01-03 }}\n\nEddie Yagin was expected to face [[Dennis Siver]] at this event. However, Yagin was forced out of the bout due to a sparring injury that landed him in the hospital with swelling around his brain, and was replaced by [[Nam Phan]].{{cite web |author=Staff |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/31610/nam-phan-replaces-eddie-yagin-meets-dennis-siver-at-ufc-on-fox-5-in-december.mma |title=Nam Phan replaces Eddie Yagin, meets Dennis Siver at UFC on FOX 5 in December |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-11-14 |access-date=2012-11-14 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121116161340/http://mmajunkie.com/news/31610/nam-phan-replaces-eddie-yagin-meets-dennis-siver-at-ufc-on-fox-5-in-december.mma |archive-date=2012-11-16 }}\n\n[[John Cholish]] was expected to face [[Yves Edwards]] at the event. However, Cholish was forced out of the bout with a groin injury and replaced by [[Jeremy Stephens]].{{cite web |author=Staff |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/2012/11/yves-edwards-vs-jeremy-stephens-rebooked-for-ufc-on-fox-5 |title=Yves Edwards vs. Jeremy Stephens rebooked for UFC on FOX 5 |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-11-24 |access-date=2012-11-24 |archive-date=2012-11-30 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121130094237/http://mmajunkie.com/news/2012/11/yves-edwards-vs-jeremy-stephens-rebooked-for-ufc-on-fox-5 |url-status=dead }}\n\n[[Lavar Johnson]] was scheduled to face [[Brendan Schaub]] at the event. However, Johnson was forced out of the bout with a groin injury and Schaub was pulled from the card altogether.{{cite web |author=Staff |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/2012/11/pulled-groin-forces-lavar-johnson-out-of-ufc-on-fox-5-bout-with-brendan-schaub |title=Pulled groin forces Lavar Johnson out of UFC on FOX 5 bout with Brendan Schaub |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-11-30 |access-date=2012-11-30 |archive-date=2012-12-02 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121202074601/http://mmajunkie.com/news/2012/11/pulled-groin-forces-lavar-johnson-out-of-ufc-on-fox-5-bout-with-brendan-schaub |url-status=dead }}\n\nAt the weigh-ins, [[Henry Martinez (fighter)|Henry Martinez]] came in heavy weighing in at 158.8 lb. Martinez was given two hours to cut to the [[Lightweight (MMA)|lightweight]] maximum of 156 pounds, but he elected instead to surrender a percentage of his fight purse to his opponent [[Daron Cruickshank]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/12/ufc-on-fox-5-live-and-official-weigh-in-results |title=UFC on Fox 5 weigh-in results |author=Staff |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=2012-12-07 |access-date=2012-12-07 |archive-date=2012-12-09 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121209012455/http://mmajunkie.com/news/2012/12/ufc-on-fox-5-live-and-official-weigh-in-results |url-status=dead }}\n\nAt the pre-weigh-in press conference, [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]], as part of merging [[Strikeforce (mixed martial arts)|Strikeforce]] champions into the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship|UFC]], officially named [[Strikeforce (mixed martial arts)|Strikeforce]] champion [[Ronda Rousey]] the [[UFC Women's Bantamweight Championship|women's bantamweight champion]] and presented her with a belt.{{Cite web The Washington State Department of Licensing's initial report did not include information on the penalty.''\n\n==See also==\n*[[List of UFC events]]\n*[[2012 in UFC]]\n\n==References==\n{{Reflist}}\n\n==External links==\n*[https://web.archive.org/web/20160916064655/http://www.ufc.com/event/Past_Events Official UFC past events page]\n*[http://www.sherdog.com/organizations/Ultimate-Fighting-Championship-2 UFC events results at Sherdog.com]\n\n{{UFC Events}}\n\n{{DEFAULTSORT:Ufc on Fox 05}}\n[[Category:Fox UFC]]\n[[Category:2012 in mixed martial arts]]\n[[Category:Mixed martial arts events in Washington (state)]]\n[[Category:Sports competitions in Seattle]]\n[[Category:2012 in sports in Washington (state)]]\n[[Category:December 2012 sports events in the United States]]\n[[Category:2012 in Seattle]]"}}}],"langlinks":[{"lang":"ja","title":"UFC on FOX 5"}]},{"pageid":36855029,"ns":0,"title":"The Ultimate Fighter: Team Carwin vs. Team Nelson","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts television series and event in 2012}}\n{{Use mdy dates|date=February 2013}}\n{{Infobox television\n|image = \n|genre = Reality, [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n|runtime = 60 minutes\n|creator = [[Frank Fertitta III]], [[Lorenzo Fertitta]], [[Dana White]]\n|starring = Dana White, [[Shane Carwin]], and [[Roy Nelson (fighter)|Roy Nelson]]\n|country = United States\n|network = [[FX (TV channel)|FX]]\n|first_aired = {{Start date|2012|09|14}}\n|last_aired =\n}}\n\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Team Carwin vs. Team Nelson''''' is the sixteenth main installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced reality television series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''.\n\nThis season reverted to the taped format, after [[The Ultimate Fighter: Live]]'s 12-week live format. The previous season saw the show be revamped. Prior seasons of the show were filmed in six weeks, edited and aired several months after filming was completed, whereas The Ultimate Fighter: Live was being filmed over thirteen weeks, featuring live fights. This season returned the old taped format, but stayed on Friday nights\n\nAbandoning the usual open tryouts for the show, the UFC and FX interviewed candidates directly, with many interviewees being former-tryout participants. On July 12, 2012, Roy Nelson and Shane Carwin were announced as the coaches for the season.\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Coaches===\n;{{color box|#F8B800|border=silver}} {{color box|black|border=silver}} '''Team Carwin''':\n* [[Shane Carwin]], head coach\n* [[Trevor Wittman]], boxing coach\n* [[Nate Marquardt]], jiu Jitsu coach\n* [[Pat Barry (fighter)|Pat Barry]], striking coach\n* [[Duane Ludwig]], striking coach\n*Leister Bowling, wrestling coach\n*Loren Landlow, sports performance coach\n\n;{{color box|#002B5F|border=silver}} {{color box|#A8ADB4|border=silver}} '''Team Nelson''':\n* [[Roy Nelson (fighter)|Roy Nelson]], head coach\n* [[Nick Diaz]]\n* [[Nate Diaz]]\n* [[Jake Shields]]\n* [[Gilbert Melendez]]\n\n===Fighters===\n*'''Team Carwin'''\n** [[Sam Alvey]], [[Bristol Marunde]], [[Mike Ricci (fighter)|Mike Ricci]], [[Neil Magny]], James Chaney, Eddy Ellis, [[Igor Araujo]], Matt Secor\n*'''Team Nelson'''\n**Dom Waters, Michael Hill, Cameron Diffley, [[Colton Smith (fighter)|Colton Smith]], Jon Manley, Nic Herron-Webb, Joey Rivera, Julian Lane\n*'''Fighters eliminated during the entry round'''\n::[[Saad Awad]], Jesse Barrett, Diego Bautista, [[Frank Camacho]], Jerel Clark, Cortez Coleman, [[Max Griffin (fighter)|Max Griffin]], Zane Kamaka, Leo Kuntz, [[Ricky Legere|Ricky Legere Jr]]., George Lockhart, Lev Magen, [[David Michaud]], Kevin Nowaczyk, Tim Ruberg, Jason South\n\n==Episodes==\n'''Episode 1: Bring It!'''\n*Dana White welcomed the 32 fighters, consisting of all welterweights, to the show at the [[Mandalay Bay Events Center]] in [[Las Vegas, Nevada]]. He introduced them to the coaches, heavyweights [[Shane Carwin]] and [[Roy Nelson (fighter)|Roy \"Big Country\" Nelson]].\n*White dismissed the fighters to pre-fight warm-ups for the elimination round.\n*The preliminary fights began:\n:'''Dom Waters''' defeated Kevin Nowaczyk by KO (punches) at 0:32 of the first round.\n:'''[[Michael Hill (Fighter)|Michael Hill]]''' defeated Lev Magen by KO (punches) at 1:27 of the first round.\n:'''[[Bristol Marunde]]''' defeated George Lockhart by submission (guillotine choke) at 2:23 of first round.\n:'''[[Mike Ricci (fighter)|Mike Ricci]]''' defeated Jason South by TKO (punches) at 1:50 of the first round.\n:'''Julian Lane''' defeated Diego Bautista by unanimous decision after two rounds.\n:'''Igor Araujo''' defeated Cortez Coleman by submission (triangle choke) at 3:16 in the third round.\n:'''James Chaney''' defeated Jerel Clark by submission (triangle choke) at 1:30 of first round.\n:'''Cameron Diffley''' defeated Zane Kamaka by submission (armbar) at 1:37 of first round.\n:'''[[Neil Magny]]''' defeated [[Frank Camacho]] by unanimous decision after three rounds.\n:'''[[Jon Manley]]''' defeated Ricky Legere Jr. by unanimous decision after two rounds.\n:'''[[Colton Smith (fighter)|Colton Smith]]''' defeated Jesse Barrett by unanimous decision after two rounds.\n:'''Matt Secor''' defeated Max Griffin by submission (triangle choke) at 2:55 in the third round.\n:'''Eddy Ellis''' defeated David Michaud by submission (arm triangle choke) at 4:29 of the second round.\n:'''Joey Rivera''' defeated [[Saad Awad]] by majority decision after two rounds.\n:'''Nic Herron-Webb''' defeated Tim Ruberg by submission (armbar) at 4:17 of the first round.\n:'''[[Sam Alvey]]''' defeated Leo Kuntz by KO (punch) at 0:47 of the first round.\n*White expressed his displeasure in some of the fights, but congratulated all of the winning fighters on making it onto the show. Carwin and Nelson then picked teams and a coin was flipped (yellow for Carwin, blue for Nelson). Nelson won the coin toss and opted to choose the first fight, so Carwin was allowed to choose the first fighter. The fighters were picked in the following order:\n\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Coach\n!1st Pick\n!2nd Pick\n!3rd Pick\n!4th Pick\n!5th Pick\n!6th Pick\n!7th Pick\n!8th Pick\n|-\n!Carwin\n|Sam Alvey\n|Bristol Marunde\n|Mike Ricci\n|Neil Magny\n|James Chaney\n|Eddy Ellis\n|Igor Araujo\n|Matt Secor\n|-\n!Nelson\n|Dom Waters\n|Michael Hill\n|Cameron Diffley\n|Colton Smith\n|Jon Manley\n|Nic Herron-Webb\n|Joey Rivera\n|Julian Lane\n|}\n\n'''Episode 2: What We Do'''\n*The sixteen victorious fighters moved into the house. Rifts immediately began to form, with Matt Secor and Julian Lane frequently arguing. Later, Lane and Colton Smith pulled a prank on members of Team Carwin.\n*Mike Ricci and Michael Hill note that they are the two only Canadian fighters on the show and befriend each other\n*Nelson announced that his team would be training once a day – as opposed to the usual format in previous seasons of two-a-day training sessions – which caused frustration amongst his team.\n*Nelson matched Diffley, a former TUF coach on TUF 7, against Magny.\n*'''Neil Magny''' defeated Cameron Diffley via unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n\n'''Episode 3: We Have Control'''\n*Nic Herron-Webb creates more household enemies when he decides that 2:00 am is the perfect time to play pool, whistle and jump rope outside people's bedrooms.\n*Julian Lane, Colton Smith and Herron-Webb take Alvey's mattress from his bedroom and float it on the pool outside. After seeing what happened, Alvey just laughed it off.\n*Carwin announced the match up of Alvey against Rivera.\n*'''Joey Rivera''' defeated Sam Alvey by majority decision (19–19, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n\n'''Episode 4: Can't Fix Stupid'''\n*Nelson had his team draw straws to determine who would fight next. Whoever drew the short straw would fight next and pick his opponent or pass the straw to a teammate.\n*Lane drew the short straw and picked Marunde to fight.\n*The fighters and coaches went to a screening of the new MMA based movie, [[Here Comes the Boom]], starring [[Kevin James]].\n*Marunde was worried about making weight. He weighed 186 lb at the fight announcement, but was able to drop down to 170 lb at the weigh in. Nelson along with Lane and Joey Rivera questioned the accuracy of those results.\n*'''Bristol Marunde''' defeated Julian Lane via unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18) after two rounds.\n*After the fight, Nelson confronted White about the controversy surrounding Marunde's weigh-in, asking if White wanted him to \"keep an eye out on the commission\" in the future. White stated that the commission's statement of Marunde making weight is true and that Nelson was being stupid.\n'''Episode 5: Unleash the Beast'''\n*Julian Lane was shown crying in the locker room after his loss talking about wanting to make a better life for his family and how he has nothing left for him.\n*Carwin asked his team who was ready to fight, (all fighters rose their hands), to determine the next fight match.\n*Matt Secor and Lane nearly came to blows after Secor farted and \"threw it at Lane.\"\n*This caused Lane to snap, claiming he would risk going home, just to fight Secor, as he had already lost his fight.\n*Carwin announced the matchup of Araujo against Herron-Webb.\n*UFC veteran and TUF alumni [[Eliot Marshall]] became a part of Team Carwin's coaching staff.\n*Team Nelson put Mike Ricci's bed on top of the gazebo in the backyard causing a verbal spat between Herron-Webb and Ricci, who was not as patient as Sam Alvey.\n*'''Igor Araujo''' defeated Nic Herron-Webb via majority decision after two rounds.\n*White and the rest of Team Nelson believed that Herron-Webb was screwed out of a third round.\n\n'''Episode 6: One Mission'''\n*Nelson complained about Nic Herron-Webb's decision loss to Igor Araujo in the previous week.\n*Members of Team Nelson ate and threw away the chicken that belonged to Matt Secor and other members of the yellow team. Secor blamed Michael Hill which caused an argument, leading to Hill calling Secor out for the next fight.\n*Despite this, Carwin chose Ellis to fight Team Nelson's Smith.\n*Carwin brought in UFC veteran Eliot Marshall and Trevor Wittman to help formulate a game plan for the fight against Ellis.\n*'''Colton Smith''' defeated Eddy Ellis by majority decision (19–18, 19–18, 19–19) after two rounds.\n*This was yet another match that White and Team Carwin believed should have gone to a third round.\n\n'''Episode 7: Summer Camp'''\n*Nelson picked his next fighter by having his team \"pick a number.\"\n*Dom Waters won, but did not want to pick an opponent, so he passed control to Hill who picked Secor.\n*TUF 1 winner [[Forrest Griffin]] came in as a guest coach to show Team Nelson some striking skills.\n*'''Michael Hill''' defeated Matt Secor by split decision after three rounds.\n*White complained about the judging once again, as the third round was deemed by those in attendance to be highly controversial.\n\n'''Episode 8: Rock 'Em, Sock 'Em'''\n*White visited the house to try to inspire the fighters after a few lackluster bouts leading to poor judges' decisions.\n*Michael Hill got drunk and challenged Neil Magny to a fight in the quarter-finals.\n*Julian Lane, in a drunken rage, then began challenging Dom Waters to a fight by continuing to plea \"let me bang, bro,\" to Hill. Despite Hill's assurance that \"[he] will let [him] bang,\" the fight never materialized and Lane ultimately cried.\n*Mike Ricci stated that people like Lane make him not want to be associated with the sport.\n*The Coaches' Challenge took place with Nelson and Carwin competing in Olympic sports such as javelin, discus and shot put. The winners of each of the 5 sports they took place in gained a 3m advantage in the decisive 400m race. Carwin easily got all of the mile advantages, before winning the race and the $20,000 reward.\n*'''Jon Manley''' defeated James Chaney via submission (guillotine choke) at 3:25 of the first round.\n*The fight was also controversial in that Chaney later admitted that he bit Manley in an attempt to escape from the submission attempt.\n\n'''Episode 9'''\n* The last match was automatically set up with Ricci matched against Waters.\n* Julian Lane throws another fit in the house when he and Colton Smith discuss what they have done in their lives.\n*'''Mike Ricci''' defeated Dom Waters via unanimous decision after three rounds.\n\n'''Episode 10'''\n* Michael Hill gets mad at Mike Ricci, as they are the two Canadians on the show, for betraying their friendship by requesting to fight him which they had previously agreed to in the finals only, and a rift forms between the two.\n* At the weigh-in, there is a verbal spat between Araujo and Smith after the BJJ blackbelt shoves the Army Ranger. Smith calls Araujo a motherfucker, enraging the Brazilian and his teammates come and restrain him.\n*'''Neil Magny''' defeated Bristol Marunde via unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18).\n*'''Colton Smith''' defeated Igor Araujo via unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18).\n\n'''Episode 11'''\n*'''Jon Manley''' defeated Joey Rivera via majority decision (19–19, 20–18, 20–18).\n*'''Mike Ricci''' defeated Michael Hill via unanimous decision (20–18, 20–18, 20–18).\n\n'''Episode 12'''\n*'''Colton Smith''' defeated Jon Manley via unanimous decision.\n*'''Mike Ricci''' defeated Neil Magny via KO (elbow) in the first round.\n*The finalists are announced to be Smith and Ricci.\n\n==Tournament Bracket==\n\n{{Round16\n\n\n||{{color box|#002B5F|border=silver}} '''Joey Rivera'''|'''MD'''|{{color Fans chose the winners of the season bonuses.\n\n*'''Fight of the Season: Colton Smith vs. Eddy Ellis'''\n*'''Knockout of the Season: Mike Ricci'''\n*'''Submission of the Season: Jon Manley'''\n\n==The Ultimate Fighter 16 Finale==\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=The Ultimate Fighter: Team Carwin vs. Team Nelson Finale\n|image=The Ultimate Fighter Team Carwin vs Team Nelson Finale Poster.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date=December 15, 2012\n|venue=[[Hard Rock Hotel and Casino (Las Vegas)|Hard Rock Hotel and Casino]]\n|city= [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]]\n|attendance= 2,427{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/12/tuf-16-finale-officially-draws-2427-attendees-for-318875-gate|title=TUF 16 Finale officially draws 2,427 attendees for $318,875 gate|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|date=2012-12-27|access-date=2013-12-27|author=Staff}}\n|gate=$569,075\n|previousevent = [[The Ultimate Fighter: The Smashes#The Ultimate Fighter: The Smashes Finale|UFC on FX: Sotiropoulos vs. Pearson]]\n|followingevent = [[UFC 155|UFC 155: dos Santos vs. Velasquez 2]]\n}}\n\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Team Carwin vs. Team Nelson Finale''''' (also known as '''''The Ultimate Fighter 16 Finale''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]. It took place on December 15, 2012, at the [[Hard Rock Hotel and Casino (Las Vegas)|Hard Rock Hotel and Casino]] in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/10/tuf-16-finale-confirmed-for-las-vegas-hard-rock-tickets-on-sale-next-week|title=TUF 16 Finale confirmed for Las Vegas Hard Rock, tickets on sale next week|work=mmajunkie.com |date=October 2, 2012 |access-date=October 2, 2012}}\n\n===Background===\n[[Shane Carwin]] was expected to face [[Roy Nelson (fighter)|Roy Nelson]] in the event's headliner. However, Carwin suffered a knee injury and was replaced by [[Matt Mitrione]].{{cite web |url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/8637068/matt-mitrione-replaces-shane-carwin-bout-roy-nelson-sources-say |title=Sources: Mitrione replaces Carwin |work=ESPN.com |access-date=2012-11-15 |date=2012-11-15 |last=Okamoto |first=Brett}}\n\nA bout between [[Melvin Guillard]] and [[Jamie Varner]] was previously linked to this card. But due to an illness, Varner had to be removed from the card thus scrapping the fight, and rebooking to [[UFC 155]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/12/tuf-16-finale-results-melvin-guillard-vs-jamie-varner|title=Varner illness cancels bout with Guillard; fight moved to UFC 155 (updated)|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|author=Matt Erickson and John Morgan|access-date=2012-12-15|date=2012-12-15}}\n\n===Results===\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (FX)}}\n{{MMAevent received $40,000 bonuses.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2012/12/tuf-16-finale-bonuses |title=TUF 16 Finale bonuses: Barry, Waldburger, Elliott, Papazian get $40K the Night: TJ Waldburger'''\n\n===Reported payout===\n\nThe following is the reported payout to the fighters as reported to the [[Nevada State Athletic Commission]]. Jared Papazian: $6,000\n\n==See also==\n* [[The Ultimate Fighter]]\n* [[List of current UFC fighters]]\n* [[List of UFC events]]\n* [[2012 in UFC]]\n\n==References==\n{{Reflist}}\n\n{{UFC Events}}\n\n{{The Ultimate Fighter}}\n\n{{DEFAULTSORT:Ultimate Fighter 16}}\n[[Category:2012 in mixed martial arts]]\n[[Category:The Ultimate Fighter seasons]]"}}}],"langlinks":[{"lang":"ja","title":"The Ultimate Fighter: Team Carwin vs. Team Nelson"}]},{"pageid":36959269,"ns":0,"title":"The Ultimate Fighter: The Smashes","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts television series and event in 2012}}\n{{Use dmy dates|date=November 2021}}\n{{Infobox television\n| image = \n| image_size = \n| caption =\n|genre = [[reality television|Reality]], [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n| runtime = 60 minutes\n| composer =\n| creator = [[Frank Fertitta III]], [[Lorenzo Fertitta]], [[Dana White]]\n| executive_producer = \n| starring = [[Dana White]], [[Ross Pearson]], and [[George Sotiropoulos]]\n| country = Australia\n| network = [[FX Australia]]
[[ESPN UK]]\n| company = \n| first_aired = {{Start date|df=yes|2012|09|19}}\n| num_episodes = \n}}\n\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: The Smashes''''' is the second foreign version of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced [[reality television]] series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''. Like The Ultimate Fighter: Brazil, this version was filmed, in its entirety, outside [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]] and consisted of 13 episodes with a live finale in Australia.{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/news/TUFSmashesCast |title=Cast Announced for TUF Smashes |work=UFC.com |date=4 September 2012 |access-date=8 September 2012}} The season tournament finale, along with the coaches fight, took place on 14 December 2012 at [[UFC on FX: Sotiropoulos vs. Pearson]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmamania.com/2012/9/7/3301419/tuf-smashes-finals-pearson-vs-sotiropoulos-targeted-for-ufc-on-fx-6 |title=TUF Smashes finals, Pearson vs. Sotiropoulos targeted for UFC on FX 6 on Dec. 14 in Australia |publisher=MMAmania.com |date=7 September 2012 |access-date=8 September 2012}}\n\nThe series was officially announced by the UFC in May 2012.{{cite web |url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/UFC-Officially-Announces-The-Ultimate-Fighter-Australia-vs-UK-Tryouts-to-Be-Held-in-June-42597 |title=UFC Officially Announces 'The Ultimate Fighter: Australia vs. UK', Tryout to be held in June |publisher=sherdog.com |date=10 May 2012 |access-date=8 September 2012}} The name ''The Smashes'' is a play on the 130-year-old cricket rivalry between England and Australia called \"[[The Ashes]].\" The UFC held open tryouts during June 2012. The casting call was for [[Lightweight (MMA)|Lightweight]] and [[Welterweight (MMA)|Welterweight]] fighters who are at least 21 years old and have a minimum of two wins in three professional fights.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/news/29171/with-tryouts-under-way-ufc-says-tuf-australia-vs-u-k-to-air-on-fx.mma |title=With tryouts underway, UFC says 'TUF: Australia vs. U.K.' to air on FX |publisher=MMAjunkie.com |date=12 June 2012 |access-date=8 September 2012}}\n\nOn 12 July 2012, Dana White announced that the coaches for the season would be [[Ross Pearson]] and [[George Sotiropoulos]] to represent the UK and Australia respectively.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/dana-white-reveals-tuf-the-smashes-coaches |title=Dana White Reveals TUF: The Smashes Coaches |date=12 July 2012 |publisher=MMAweekly.com |access-date=8 September 2012}}\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Coaches===\n{{Col-begin}}\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{flagicon|AUS}} '''Team Australia'''\n*[[George Sotiropoulos]], Head Coach\n*Nick Kara\n*Charles Blanchard\n*Leonard Gabriel Jr.\n*[[Nathan Coy]]\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{flagicon|UK}} '''Team UK'''\n*[[Ross Pearson]], Head Coach\n*[[Dean Amasinger]]\n*Jimmy Harbison\n*Jason Soares\n*Erin Beach\n\n{{col-end}}\n\n===Fighters===\n;{{flagicon|AUS}} '''Team Australia'''\n*'''Welterweights'''\n**[[Benny Alloway]], Xavier Lucas, Manny Rodriguez (replaced by James Vainikolo due to injury), and [[Robert Whittaker (fighter)|Robert Whittaker]].\n*'''Lightweights'''\n**Grant Blackler, Patrick Iodice, [[Richie Vaculik]], and Ben Wall.\n\n;{{flagicon|UK}} '''Team UK'''\n*'''Welterweights'''\n**Luke Newman, Bola Omoyele, Valentino Petrescu, and [[Brad Scott (fighter)|Brad Scott]].\n*'''Lightweights'''\n**Colin Fletcher, [[Norman Parke]], Michael Pastou (replaced by [[Brendan Loughnane]] due to injury), and [[Mike Wilkinson (fighter)|Mike Wilkinson]].\n\n==Episodes==\n'''Episode 1: National Pride'''\n*All 16 fighters moved into the house, based in Australia, and were introduced to [[Dana White]] and their respective coaches.\n*Team Australia won the coin toss, meaning they got to pick the first fight; however, Team UK gained the right to state which weight class would compete.\n*Team UK's Michael Pastou sustained a bicep injury in sparring and was later told that the injury would most likely need surgery\n*Benny Alloway defeated Valentino Petrescu via TKO (punches) at 0:42 of round 2.\n\n'''Episode 2: Freak or Unique'''\n*The fighters got a surprise visit from UFC Heavyweight Champion [[Junior dos Santos]].\n*Team UK's Michael Pastou was forced out of the competition due to his bicep injury, and was replaced by [[Brendan Loughnane]].\n*During training Team UK's Colin Fletcher decided to climb the scaffolding and put a fake mustache and eyepatch on George Sotiropoulos poster.\n*With Team Australia in control, George puts Ben Wall against Colin Fletcher.\n*Colin Fletcher defeated Ben Wall via unanimous decision.\n\n'''Episode 3: Wasabi'''\n*Team Australia retaliate to Team UK by cutting out the head of the Ross Pearson poster and sticking it on the body of [[Arianny Celeste]].\n*Ross Pearson took Team UK outside to train to get in touch with nature in the rain.\n*Colin Fletcher and Ben Wall are persuaded to snort lines of Wasabi and Peri Peri Sauce.\n*With Team UK in control, Pearson puts Luke Newman against Robert Whittaker.\n*Robert Whittaker defeated Luke Newman via KO at 0:19 of round 1.\n\n'''Episode 4: Pick Me!'''\n*Team Australia tackle the outdoors by undertaking a long jog on the beach and doing some uphill sprints.\n*Team Australia are fed up with Team UK's lack of cleanliness.\n*With Team Australia in control, George puts Richie Vaculik against Norman Parke.\n*Colin Fletcher does naked laps around the tennis court.\n*The pranks get out of hand when Team Australia effortlessly put training mats against Team UK's changing room door. Team UK respond by tying a bucket full of coffee, water and honey to the doorknob so that it is poured onto an unsuspecting victim. Team Australia get back at them by pouring a bucket of smelly water onto Team UK's couches.\n*Norman Parke defeats Richie Vaculik by unanimous decision.\n\n'''Episode 5: Fight for Your Family'''\n*Colin Fletcher defaces a poster of George Sotiropoulos in the house.\n*Team Australia are not impressed with the house antics from Team UK which include food fights and more naked runs.\n*With Team UK in control, Pearson puts Bola Omoyele against Manny Rodriguez.\n*Luke Newman has thoughts of leaving the show and returning home as he can't train with his current medical suspension. With his friend Bola Omoyele due to fight next, he needs convincing from his teammates and coaches before finally deciding to stay on and support his team.\n*Manny Rodriguez defeats Bola Omoyele via [[North–south position|north-south choke]] at 4:07 of round 1.\n\n'''Episode 6: True Warrior'''\n*Team Australia's Manny Rodriguez was forced out of the competition after suffering a fractured leg in his previous bout, and was replaced by James Vainikolo.\n*With Team Australia in control, George puts Patrick Iodice against [[Brendan Loughnane]].\n*Colin Fletcher dresses as the new replacement for Team Australia.\n* [[Brendan Loughnane]] defeats Patrick Iodice by unanimous decision.\n\n'''Episode 7: Unwise Words'''\n*Ben Wall joins Team UK in a food fight.\n*Pearson announces the last welterweight quarter-final fight; Brad Scott vs. Xavier Lucas\n*The UFC has decided to have James Vainikolo fight his way into the semi-finals. Team Australia chooses which UK fighter will fight him.\n*Team UK reads through Brad Scott's personal diary leaving many teammates upset.\n*UFC [[Light heavyweight (MMA)|Light Heavyweight]], [[James Te Huna]], visits Team Australia before their fight.\n*Brad Scott defeats Xavier Lucas by unanimous decision.\n\n'''Episode 8: Phone Home'''\n*The final lightweight quarter-final is announced, Grant Blackler vs. Mike Wilkinson.\n*Dean Amasinger (a UK assistant coach) walked in on an Australian training session to find a book with valuable info on all members of Team UK. Sotiropoulos begins ordering Amasinger to leave, Pearson then walks in to defend Amasinger.\n*The book is then revealed as stolen by members of Team Australian and is given back to the Team UK coaches.\n*It is announced that James Vainikolo will not fight a member of Team UK, but instead, would fight Xavier Lucas, which angers UK welterweight Valentino Petrescu.\n*Dana White scolds Team UK for using a cell phone to call home, and send text messages of fight results.\n*Vainikolo could not make weight and the fight was called off, causing Xavier Lucas to move into the semifinals.\n*Mike Wilkinson defeats Grant Blackler via Submission of round 1.\n\n'''Episode 9: Easy Route'''\n*Brad Scott defeats Benny Alloway via split decision after 3 rounds\n\n'''Episode 10: Smash it UP'''\n*Norman Parke defeats Brendan Loughane via unanimous decision after 3 rounds\n\n'''Episode 11: Friendly Fire'''\n*Robert Whittaker defeats Xavier Lucas via TKO (punches) in round 1\n\n'''Episode 12: Little and Large'''\n*Colin Fletcher defeats Richie Vaculik via submission (keylock) in round 2\n\n==Welterweight bracket==\n{{Round8\n\n\n||{{flagicon|AUS}} '''Robert Whittaker'''|'''KO'''|{{flagicon|UK}} Luke Newman|1\n||{{flagicon|AUS}} '''Manny Rodriguez*'''|'''SUB'''|{{flagicon|UK}} Bola Omoyele|1\n||{{flagicon|AUS}} Xavier Lucas|2|{{flagicon|UK}} '''Brad Scott'''|'''UD'''\n||{{flagicon|AUS}} '''Benny Alloway'''|'''TKO'''|{{flagicon|UK}} Valentino Petrescu|2\n\n||{{flagicon|AUS}} '''Robert Whittaker'''|'''TKO'''|{{flagicon|AUS}} Xavier Lucas**|1\n||{{flagicon|UK}} '''Brad Scott'''|'''SD'''|{{flagicon|AUS}} Benny Alloway|3\n\n||{{flagicon|AUS}} '''Robert Whittaker''' |'''UD'''|{{flagicon|UK}} Brad Scott|3\n|widescore=yes|3rdplace=no| Round|RD3=Finale}}\n\n''*James Vainikolo was due to fight Xavier Lucas for a spot in the next round to replace the injured Manny Rodriguez. '''Submission of the Season: Colin Fletcher'''\n\n==The Ultimate Fighter: The Smashes Finale==\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=UFC on FX: Sotiropoulos vs. Pearson\n|image=The smashes finale.png\n|date=
